# Design-system gaps & conflicts

Log required by `mentor-settings-build-spec.md` (§2, §7). One row per token,
component state, spacing/motion value, or content rule the spec doesn't resolve.
**Figma values win on conflict**; nothing here was invented silently.

Contrast ratios are measured (WCAG 2.2 relative luminance), on the surface named.

| # | Token / pattern | Where needed | Interim value used | Recommendation | Severity |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `text/tertiary` `#a0998d` — **2.57:1 on s1, fails AA** (spec §2 flag confirmed) | Inactive tab labels (16px regular), any tertiary text | Not used for informational text anywhere in Phase 0; reserved for decorative/disabled-redundant only | Darken the tertiary token to ≥4.5:1 (e.g. `#797774` → still see row 2, or `#6e6b66`) | **Serious** |
| 2 | Muted body `#797774` — **measured 4.06:1 on s1** (spec asked to verify; fails 4.5:1 normal-text AA, passes 3:1 large/UI) | Card status lines, preview labels; spec's interim rule suggested it for informational text | Kept ONLY for 14px card status lines that restate/summarize the section title (redundant-adjacent); **inactive tab labels use `text/secondary #58534b` (6.94:1) instead**, since a tab label is the control's sole carrier | **RESOLVED (Phase 8): `--text-muted` darkened to `#6e6b67` — measured 4.82:1 on s1, 5.30:1 on white.** Figma tertiary/muted tokens should adopt this value | ~~Serious~~ Resolved |

| 125 | PRODUCT-WIDE DARK MODE on Bloom tokens (user-mandated). ROOT CAUSE of "the switch only affects the menu": the theme plumbing was always correct (`.dark` on `<html>`, persisted, System follows OS live) but dark VALUES existed only for the stock shadcn scaffold in globals.css — generic neutral `oklch()` greys — while `settings-tokens.css`, which carries the ~60 tokens the product actually paints with (548 usages), had `:root` only. So shadcn chrome (popover/menu/sonner) flipped and every `var(--surface-*)` panel stayed light. FIX: a full `.dark` block in settings-tokens.css mirroring every colour token. Ladder is ground `#0e0d0c` (warmGrey/950) → panel `#272421` (900) → card `#322f2c` = warmGrey/900 + 5% white, using BLOOM'S OWN `stateLayers/primary` mechanic rather than an invented off-ramp step — warmGrey/800 `#3f3b36` was tried first and dropped muted text to 3.93:1. Measured on card/panel: primary 12.9/15.4, secondary 6.0/7.2, muted 4.7/5.5, focus (periwinkle/300) 4.7, brand-ink 5.7 — all ≥4.5; tertiary 3.4 stays decorative-only as in light. NEW TOKEN FAMILIES: `--status-*` (5 chip families, light values byte-identical to the former raw `-100/-800` Tailwind pairs — resolves #70/#117), `--tint-{faint,hover,press}` (interaction state layers: black wash in light, WHITE in dark — hard-coded `bg-black/[0.04]` hovers are invisible on dark and are what makes an unthemed dark mode feel dead; 5 ad-hoc opacities normalised to 3 levels, max 1% alpha change), `--text-inverse` (ink on a fill that itself inverts — the pill button/tooltip/selected chip filled with `--text-primary` + hard-coded `text-white` went white-on-white in dark), `--brand-ink` (tangelo/600 light, /300 dark: pure `#ff5001` is only ~3.1:1 on white), `--chart-engagement` (`#2f6fed` is 4.55:1 on the white card but 2.93:1 on the dark one → lifts to `#5b8ff9`). ALERT FAMILIES REBALANCED: Bloom's raw *Highlight steps INVERTED the hierarchy (warning sunshine/900 read at 2.14:1 presence vs panel while critical tangelo/900 sat at 1.01 — the mildest state twice as loud as the most severe); retuned to near-equal presence (1.23/1.30/1.30) so HUE carries urgency and no band physically shouts. ELEVATION: Bloom's Elevation collection is single-mode pure-black at 7–9%, invisible on dark — shadows are DEEPENED locally (black 30–65%) and separation leans on surface contrast + a white-10% contour. SHADCN LAYER now aliases the semantic tokens, but ONLY for the 6 whose light values are byte-identical; 8 differ (`--ring` #ff5001 vs #716a60 rgbΔ173, `--background` #faf6ef vs #e5e0d2 Δ42 materially) and keep their light hex with an explicit dark value — aliasing those would have silently restyled light mode. This unification also FIXES the dark-on-dark Select (shadcn `--card` flipped while `--text-primary` did not; verified `oklch(0.205)` = Lab L* 7.782, exactly the measured value) plus its 48px-instead-of-36px height (a `data-[size=default]:h-12` variant out-specified callers' `h-9`; now a plain `h-12` so tailwind-merge lets callers win). NO-FLASH: a synchronous inline `<script>` in `<head>` sets `.dark` pre-paint. next/script `beforeInteractive` was tried and REJECTED — in App Router it defers into `self.__next_s`, drained after parse, i.e. after the flash; React 19 logs a dev-only "script tag while rendering React component" notice for the raw tag, which is the accepted trade. TOASTS: sonner called `useTheme()` from next-themes but NO ThemeProvider exists, so it always fell back to "system" and followed the OS, not the in-app switch → now reads `useAppearance()`. TEEN PHONE PREVIEWS ARE FIXED-IDENTITY and deliberately do NOT theme (they mock what teens see; teens don't share staff appearance) — 5 files annotated. Light mode verified BYTE-IDENTICAL across 28 pre-existing token values. Dark VALUES ARE PROVISIONAL: Bloom's dark mode is real (collection "Color Tokens", 65 vars × Light/Dark) but read-only Figma tools resolve one mode at a time and the local Jul-7 export has drifted (surface/s0 + button/border/subtle re-aliased, ramps renamed, ~11 tokens with no documented dark value), so these are built from Bloom's LIVE-VERIFIED PRIMITIVE RAMPS via its documented dark mapping | Whole staff app | settings-tokens.css (.dark + 4 new families) + globals.css + layout.tsx + ui/{select,sonner}.tsx + ~20 staff components | Swap in exact Bloom dark hexes (one token block) once a Dark-mode frame URL or a read-only use_figma dump is available; second pass for mentor-facing onboarding/hub/invite (~158 hardcoded colours + doodle art); decide if the record's elevation doctrine should adopt the white-overlay mechanic in light too | Major |

GEOMETRY: `seg` is the segment height and the track adds its 2px inset per side ⇒ md 32+4=36px track / 32px thumb (Bloom spec); sm 28+4=32px preserves the two pre-existing consumers. Radius is pill at both levels, which satisfies "container = thumb + inset" by construction (32/2+2 = 36/2 = 18px). TWO DEFECTS I SHIPPED AND CAUGHT BY MEASURING: (a) first pass put h-9 on the SEGMENT, making the track 40px not 36px — the inset stacks on top; (b) the thumb boundary was carried by --shadow-tile alone at 1.10:1 fill-to-fill, so --border-contour was added to reinforce the edge. MEASURED: selected label 16.64:1 light / 13.30:1 dark; unselected 6.94:1 / 7.15:1; hit area 32px visual + 6px per side = 44px. NO MOTION TOKENS EXIST in the token layer — duration/easing stay hardcoded (200ms, ease-out) rather than minting a token set in a scoped pass; flagged. No mutation exists in the module by construction. `author` is a discriminated union (person|system|ai) rendered on EVERY row, because a reader must never have to infer whether a rationale came from a clinician or the intake mapping. `amends` makes chains VISIBLE both ways: the superseded entry keeps rendering with "Amended on [date]" and the correction says "Amends the [date] entry", both as jumps that CLEAR the active filter first (a chain's two halves can land under different chips). `visibility: "all_staff"` is the only value today — the field exists now so a clinical-only class is a data change, not a refactor of every consumer. (2) the summary card derived the presentation by string-splitting `result`, so a review whose result reads "F41.1 — unchanged" printed that whole phrase as the code; presentation events now carry an explicit `code` and `result` stays prose. (3) t13's PHQ-9 breakdown was a 5-item slice of another teen's, summing to 9 against a stated 8 — a truncated instrument isn't a PHQ-9 and the expanded rows have to add up to the row above them. (4) I hardcoded an escalation onto t15 by copying t9's reason, while `DemoTeen.escalation` ALREADY IS the escalation record (it drives the mentee row's critical flag) — so the Care tab claimed an escalation the mentee row didn't show, on a teen with none on file. Escalation events now DERIVE from that field; only t9 has one, and its timeline is a single derived row. (5) the assign affordance was gated on an EMPTY TIMELINE rather than on the absence of a survey, which hid it from exactly the teen who needs it — t9 carries an escalation and no survey. GATING: the only action affordance in Prompt A is "Assign first survey" in the pre-first-survey state, supervisor tier and above (`canActOnCare`), ABSENT below rather than disabled — zero disabled/aria-disabled elements in the Care region at mentor tier, where the only buttons are the 5 read-only filter chips. Focus ring verified with real Tab keypresses: 2px rgb(113,106,96) at 2px offset, `:focus-visible` true. Enter/Space activation could NOT be exercised — the browser harness dispatches keydown without synthesizing button activation, confirmed against a control button on the same page; both controls are native `<button type="button">`, so the UA owns it. PRE-FIRST-SURVEY is treated as the common case, not an edge: "Not yet assigned" rather than em dashes, a designed timeline empty state, and the Mentorship rail still populated so the page reads as "nothing has happened yet" and never as a failed load. | 142 | PRESENTATION SPINE, ACTION SLOT, CADENCE (MO-9.1, Prompt B) — Phases 1, 2 and 4. PHASE 3 IS NOT BUILT; see the blocker note below. SPINE: `PresentationCard` leads the column — statement at 22px display weight, code beneath, then provenance. CLINICAL DATES SUSPEND THE ELAPSED-TIME STANDARD and say so in a comment: "assigned 4 months ago" is unusable to anyone reconciling this record against an outside provider's notes, and a diagnosis date is a fact of record, not a recency signal. The cadence COUNTDOWN keeps elapsed framing because a countdown is a distance — so Cadence deliberately carries both forms. TWO MODES: resting (spine + Cadence paired) and review-pending (`ActionSlot` takes the top, the spine demotes to `PresentationReference` reading on-record → recommended). ACTION SLOT is a GENERIC shell — tinted surface, header, body, footer — that knows nothing about presentations; the presentation review is its first consumer and treatment-plan/goal/crisis consumers reuse it untouched. Warm family, not critical (a scheduled review is expected work; critical stays reserved for escalations), and `box-shadow: none` in both themes: the Figma exploration's floating white box is the only shadowed element on that page and it breaks the surface system. NOTHING PENDING RENDERS NOTHING — no "all caught up" card. APPROVE IS GATED ON A NON-WHITESPACE PROVIDER NOTE, with the requirement in helper text BEFORE the reach for the button rather than as a post-click error; verified that a spaces-only note keeps it disabled. Approving APPENDS (store v29 `careReviews`, keyed by review id, with NO update or delete setter — the record is append-only by construction) and the entry carries the note as its providerNote and the deciding supervisor as author. FIVE DEFECTS CAUGHT BY MEASURING: (1) the locked survey read GAD-7 beside a Last-assessment row reading PHQ-9, because `cadenceFor` took "the first assessment" off the end of an UNSORTED array — now sorted explicitly, and the locked instrument is the one the series actually tracks. (2) a presentation whose code had been CHANGED reported "assigned <the change date> · not yet reviewed", erasing eleven days of history and denying the review that had plainly happened — `presentationFor` now walks the amendment chain back to the original assignment and treats a change/review entry as the review date. (3) the appended author rendered "Supervisor · Supervisor" from one field doing two jobs; split into `actor` (name) + `actorRole` (tier). (4) after an approval the quarterly clock still read "overdue by 5 days", because store decisions append to the END of the event array and an unsorted `find` kept returning the 95-day-old fixture review. (5) in DARK mode the provider-note label and its required-field helper text sat on the warm tint at 4.22:1 — below AA on the one line that must be read; that block moved onto a card panel (worst text in the slot is now 5.30:1 light / 4.71:1 dark). Also fixed: the paired lockup split at `md`, which at 1024 left ~290px per card and wrapped a clinical statement over three lines — now `xl` (two 443px cards, 24px gap, statement on two lines at 1440). GATING VERIFIED ACROSS THE MATRIX: the review payload is never even FETCHED below supervisor tier (`canAct ? pendingReviewFor(...) : null`), so mentor and lead-mentor render the RESTING page on a record with a pending review — the nine requested states are really 3 tiers x 2 modes where the lower two tiers have one mode, because review-pending is a supervisor-only condition. Zero disabled or aria-disabled controls in the content column at mentor or lead tier on every teen checked; the only controls there are the 5 read-only filter chips and the timeline disclosures. The disabled Approve is a WITHIN-tier state (note not yet written), not a cross-tier gate, and its #b8b2a9 fill is the pre-existing recorded exemption (gaps.md #16, WCAG 1.4.3 exempts inactive controls). Every change goes through a confirm naming which adjacent surface moves. LOCK is a new concept and deliberately NOT deactivation: deactivated means not an active user (reversible); locked means they cannot sign up AGAIN, because their email and phone are blocked, so the block outlives the account. It is therefore only offered ONCE DEACTIVATED — blocking an active user's contact details would be incoherent — and the button explains why it is unavailable rather than sitting there inert. Note this is a STATE precondition inside one tier, not a permission gate, which is why a disabled-with-explanation is right here where the Care page's absent-not-disabled rule applies to tiers. Unlock exists: blocking the wrong email with no remedy is a worse failure than the one locking prevents. The confirm and the locked line both NAME the blocked identifiers. PARENT DETAILS on the teen Account tab, below their own facts: name, phone, email, consent, opt-out reason. Consent is the most consequential field on the page — it governs what may be sent about a minor — so it goes through a confirm rather than saving as you type like the text fields. The Figma labels it "Parent Consent (EDIT WITH CARE)"; the shouted parenthetical became a caution line UNDER the field, because the copy law is sentence case and a warning belongs beside the control rather than inside the label a screen reader announces. OPT-OUT REASON renders read-only with an explanation until consent is declined or withdrawn — offering it beside "Consent given" invites a sentence that contradicts the field above it. All fields start EMPTY (TODO-DATA): an invented parent name on a minor's consent record would be worse than a blank. | 149 | PRESENCE MOVED TO THE AVATAR; role badge is a role label again. The badge's dot meant LIFECYCLE STANDING, so the pill carried two different facts at once — and the standing half was already duplicated, since any non-default state renders as its own word-chip beside it. Removing the dot loses nothing: active is the default and defaults whisper. Presence now sits bottom-right of the avatar, ringed in the card surface so it reads as ON the avatar, with `role="img"` + "Online now" so it is never colour alone. Applied to all three header implementations (UserRecordShell, its two consumers, and the live record's inline header); the dead `TEEN_STATUS_DOT` map and the `headerDotFor` imports that only fed the badge are gone. PRESENCE AND STANDING ARE NOW INDEPENDENT, which is the point — verified on t15: paused AND online at once, the chip saying "Paused" and the avatar dot saying online. Offline renders NOTHING (t14: no dot). TODO-DATA, REPORTED: presence does not exist upstream. Audited — every "online" in the codebase refers to first-message LANES or availability HOURS, neither of which is "is this person here now". So `isOnlineNow` is a small fixture set, deliberately NOT derived from message recency: "messaged today" is not "online now", and a green dot on a teen who isn't there is a claim a mentor could act on. ALSO FIXED, from a console error the user surfaced: the FOURTH instance of the pressable-row hazard. `RecordRow`'s press branch wrapped the whole row in a <button>, so `SpotifyChip`'s copy button and its "open in Spotify" link were <button>-in-<button> and <a>-in-<button> — invalid HTML, a hydration error, and both clicks swallowed into the row's navigation. | 184 | THE WARM BAND TRIAD — three new tokens, because the system had none and nothing composed into one. The routine band mixed `--text-primary` into white and measured a chroma spread of **ZERO**: a dead neutral grey on a warm cream canvas, which is what "disabled" looks like. Why the panel looked worse than the cards: a white card gets 1.319:1 of SURFACE contrast doing the boundary work, but the panel's footer fill (`--surface-s1`) sits at only 1.20:1 against the canvas, so there was almost no surface contrast left and the invisible contour was the only thing there. At 16% the edge composites to `rgb(197,193,181)` — 1.365:1, just past the surface contrast it accompanies, so the line reads about as strongly as the boundary it draws rather than disappearing behind it. Restructured so the header row and the body are SIBLINGS: a fixed `h-14` header holding score · band chip · instrument+date on one baseline (`items-baseline`, no stacking) with the control as its own flex child, and the body beneath a hairline below it. Nothing in the header can move when the body appears because the body is not in it. MEASURED AFTER: header **56px collapsed and 56px expanded**, disclosure moved **dx 0 / dy 0**, score moved **dx 0 / dy 0**. | 223 | NO TOAST-WITH-UNDO PATTERN, AND THE TOAST LIBRARY CANNOT MEET §7's FOCUS REQUIREMENT ON ITS OWN. §5 specifies a 6s toast with an Undo action and §7 adds "undo toast reachable by keyboard before timeout, pause timer on focus". sonner 2.0.7 pauses a toast's timer on exactly three conditions — `expanded || interacting || isDocumentHidden` — all pointer- or visibility-driven. **KEYBOARD FOCUS PAUSES NOTHING**, so a reader tabbing toward Undo watches it vanish mid-reach: the one group that needs the window most is the one group that does not get it. Two obvious fixes both fail. (a) `duration: Infinity` plus `toast.dismiss()` on commit: sonner wraps `dismiss` in a `requestAnimationFrame`, so a tab that is not painting never retires the toast and Undo outlives the window it belongs to. (b) Re-issuing the toast with a longer duration: a toast's duration is read into a `useRef` at mount, so an in-place update cannot move a live timer. IMPLEMENTED: focus is translated into the presence signal sonner already understands — a `focusin` inside the toaster pauses `resolveWithUndo`'s own window AND dispatches the pointer-presence event that sets `expanded`, and `focusout` releases both. Both clocks pause together, because the Undo button and the thing it undoes must not disagree about how long is left. `ResolveHandle` gained `pause()` / `resume()` / `remainingMs()`, with the property that makes it safe asserted in tests: a pause never GRANTS time, it preserves what was left. | Tasks resolve path; any future undo toast | `src/lib/tasks/resolve.ts` pause/resume; the focus effect in `src/components/tasks/tasks-page.tsx`; 6 tests in `resolve.test.ts` | Define an undo-toast pattern with a focus contract, and either patch/replace sonner or bless the presence-signal adapter | Warning |

| 232 | THE SURFACE LADDER DOES NOT FLIP WITH THE THEME, SO "A STEP ABOVE THE SURFACE" CANNOT BE BUILT FROM IT. A10 item 5 asks an unread row to take "row surface fill at a subtle step above default". Built from `--surface-s1` it was correct in light and INVERTED in dark: `--surface-s1` is #f6f4ef against a #ffffff card in light (a step down in luminance, reading as a lift on a white ground) but #272421 against a #322f2c card in dark — genuinely darker, so an unread row RECESSED. The `--tint-*` family is the one that flips polarity (black at 2/4/6% in light, white at 4/6/9% in dark), so the unread wash is `--tint-faint` and hover stays `--tint-hover`. THAT ALSO YIELDS A MONOTONIC FOUR-STATE LADDER with no collisions, because hover is painted ON TOP of the resting wash rather than swapped in — measured luminance, light: 1.000 / 0.956 / 0.913 / 0.871 for read / unread / read-hovered / unread-hovered; dark: 0.0289 / 0.0388 / 0.0444 / 0.0552. Text on the unread wash measures 15.95:1 light and 11.82:1 dark. GENERAL RULE WORTH ADOPTING: any "one step up from where you are" treatment must come from the tint family, never the surface ladder — the surface ladder is absolute positions, the tints are relative offsets. | Activity unread row; any future raised-row state | `src/components/activity/activity-row.tsx`; measurements in the A10 report | State the rule in the design system, or add a polarity-flipping raised-surface token | Warning |

The Risk Flagging Escalation Playbook supplies the missing structure: TWENTY categories across three response tiers, transcribed into `lib/record/risk-taxonomy.ts` with the five pre-existing wire values unchanged. It yields TWO independent axes — TIER (how fast a supervisor must respond) and THEME (what the flag is clinically about: self-harm, harm to others, abuse or home environment, substance use, eating or body image, psychotic symptoms, bullying, assessment scores). The THEME axis is what the pattern block groups on, so a reading says "two of them involving abuse or the home environment" — the clinically meaningful version of the brief's example. THREE DELIBERATE DEPARTURES FROM THE PLAYBOOK, each because a governing rule wins: (1) its tiers are named by population percentile ("Top 1%", "Top 5%") and those names appear NOWHERE in the product — a percentile attached to a child is a risk score, and "a Top 1% teen" is the sentence that phrasing produces; the tiers are renamed by the response they require. (2) Its 🔴🔵🟢 colour coding is not carried over — a colour-coded person is out of bounds and colour alone fails WCAG 1.4.1 regardless. (3) `GROOMING_BEHAVIOR` predates the playbook and is not one of its twenty; it keeps its label and theme and its tier is "unassigned", because assigning one would be inventing clinical routing. ALSO NOTED, NOT RESOLVED: the playbook's own General-tier timing is internally inconsistent — the summary table says "Within 7 days", Part 3 says "within 48 hours". No SLA is encoded here; response clocks belong to the escalation workflow. | Flag taxonomy; Care pattern grouping | `src/lib/record/risk-taxonomy.ts`; `CATEGORY_LABEL` now derives from it; 20 tests | Settle the General-tier timing, and say whether grooming should be assigned a tier | Warning |

It is the same defect the Tasks worklist hit with coalesced group children (row 216). FIXED by coalescing flags per day into one mark carrying the count, in the label as well as on the mark — "4 flags · August 18, 2026" — with the mark's event id pointing at the FIRST flag of the day so activating it lands the reader at the top of the group rather than inside it. Assessments and presentation changes deliberately do NOT coalesce: two of either on one day does not occur, and adding the machinery would be solving a problem with no instances. | 250 | THE HARNESS CANNOT USE `useSearchParams`, AND CANNOT USE THE ESCAPE HATCH EITHER. Standing rule 3 forbids `useSearchParams` (it wants a Suspense boundary, and a page-root Suspense silently breaks hard-load hydration here) and prescribes the `use(searchParams)` PROP pattern instead. THE TRADE, stated rather than hidden: the bar does not follow a browser back/forward step. The strip is `overflow-x-auto` with the scrollbar hidden, and the comment above it records why that must stay: authoring only `overflow-x` silently made the Y axis scrollable too (per the CSS overflow spec, when one axis is `visible` and the other is not, `visible` computes to `auto`), and a `-bottom-px` active indicator gave it 1px to report — clientHeight 44, scrollHeight 45, and a 15px vertical scrollbar eating the strip's width. THAT REASONING IS UNTOUCHED and re-verified after this change: clientHeight 44, scrollHeight 44, `overflow-y: hidden`, no scrollbar. What is added is the discoverability the hiding cost (Nielsen #6): position-aware edge fades, drawn only on a side that actually has hidden content (verified: at scrollLeft 0 the fades read [0,1], mid-scroll [1,1], at the end [1,0]), plus the strip scrolling the ACTIVE tab into view. The second half matters more than the first — a record is deep-linked by tab, and at 390px roughly two of six tabs fit, so arriving on `?tab=account` previously showed Overview and Mentorship with the tab the reader was on scrolled out of sight. `scrollLeft` is written directly rather than calling `scrollIntoView`, because scrollIntoView walks every scrollable ancestor and also scrolled the record panel, landing the reader mid-page with the identity header pushed off the top. Verified `actVis: true` on all 16 reachable tab-views at 390. | 259 | A LIVE HYDRATION MISMATCH ON THE TEEN RECORD, CAUSED BY A LAZY `useState` INITIALISER READING THE CLOCK. THE VALUES DRIFT BETWEEN RELOADS — 18.6837% then 18.6829% then 18.6822%, falling steadily — which is what identifies the cause: the positions derive from a timestamp that differs between server render and client hydration. `admin/person/[id]/page.tsx:101` is `const [now] = useState(() => Date.now())`, carrying a comment explaining it exists to satisfy `react-hooks/purity` because `Date.now()` in render scope is impure. IT DOES SATISFY THE LINT AND IT DOES NOT FIX THE PROBLEM: a lazy initialiser runs during the SERVER render too, and again on the client at hydration, so anything derived from `now` is guaranteed to differ. The visible symptom is confined to four `aria-hidden` decorative labels, but `now` also feeds assessment dates, elapsed-time figures, cadence and the Care summary, so the inconsistency is broader than what React happens to report. | 266 | THE IN-APP BROWSER PANE CANNOT VERIFY SCROLL-DRIVEN UI AT ALL, WHICH COST AN HOUR AND A WRONG DIAGNOSIS. Extends row 228. Measured directly: `document.hidden === true` and `visibilityState === "hidden"`, and in that state **scroll events never fire (0 of 3 dispatched), ResizeObserver never fires (0 callbacks), and `requestAnimationFrame` never runs**. The consequences compound: (a) a `useEdgeOverflow`-style hook never re-measures, so its state is frozen at whatever the initial layout effect computed; (b) any `transition-*` is stuck at its START value forever, so `getComputedStyle(el).opacity` reads 0 on an element whose inline style says 1 — an inline `el.style.opacity = '1'` written from the console ALSO read back 0. THE TRAP: (b) makes correct code look broken in a way that points at the wrong culprit. I diagnosed the tab strip's `opacity-0`/`opacity-100` class pair as failing Tailwind's scan and rewrote it, before finding that the DOM class was right all along and only the transition was frozen. THE RULE THIS BUYS: to verify a scroll affordance in this pane, read `el.style.*` or the className, NEVER `getComputedStyle` of a transitioned property — and drive state by calling the handler, not by setting `scrollLeft` and waiting for an event that will not arrive. `scrollLeft` DOES stick (150 and 300 both read back), so geometry is measurable; only the events are not. | Verification method for any scroll-driven affordance | Measured on `/admin/person/s-stephen?tab=supervision`; `ui/scroll-affordance.tsx` carries the note | None — a method note. Verify transitions in a real browser tab | Info |
| 267 | DATAVIEW: THE THRESHOLD IS A CONTAINER QUERY, AND THAT IS THE DECISION THE WHOLE PRIMITIVE RESTS ON. RC-3's four tables needed a table above some width and a card list below it. TWO THRESHOLDS, AS LITERAL CLASS PAIRS: Tailwind scans source text, so a threshold interpolated from a prop generates no CSS at all; `45rem` (720px, for a 640px table) and `60rem` (960px, for a table whose pinned columns already sum past 760px) are written out and callers pick by key. FOUR SLOTS carry the per-column priority: `primary` (the card's title), `secondary` (the middot-joined meta line), `trailing` (the right-hand cluster), `detail` (table only — a recorded product decision, not a `hidden lg:` dodge). (1) BROWSER BACK AND SWIPE-BACK: tab changes use `router.replace`, so they do not push history — Back from a dirty Account tab does not return to the previous tab, it LEAVES THE PAGE, and the most likely gesture on a phone was the least guarded one. FIXED as `lib/use-unsaved-changes-guard.ts`, page-scoped rather than global — the listeners exist only while the form is dirty, so there is no app-wide behaviour change. Next 16's `<Link onNavigate>` was considered and rejected: the links that leave this page live in `AppShell`, `settings/sidebar.tsx` and `bottom-nav.tsx`, all shared by every route, so wiring it would make the app shell know about one page's form state. A capture-phase click listener keeps the shell ignorant. `beforeunload` is used for path 3 ONLY, because the browser owns that moment and will not be styled; every in-app path gets the existing dialog.
