| 276 | `--muted-foreground` WAS FIXED IN DARK MODE AND LEFT RAW IN LIGHT — one token, one layer, and the single largest a11y defect in the codebase. The Phase 8 axe baseline returned 852 `color-contrast` violations across 224 view-viewports, and the sampled nodes are all one pair: `#797774` on `#ffffff` at **4.464:1**, failing AA by 0.04. There are TWO token layers for muted ink. `settings-tokens.css:28` `--text-muted: #6e6b67` was already audited and darkened ("Darkened from the observed #797774 (4.06:1, failed…)"). `globals.css` then defines the shadcn alias twice: `.dark` correctly says `--muted-foreground: var(--text-muted)`, and `:root` still says `--muted-foreground: #797774`. So the fix was applied to dark mode and not to light, and 91 `text-muted-foreground` call sites inherit the raw hex in the mode almost everyone uses. ALIASING LIGHT `:root` TO `var(--text-muted)` IS NOT THE WHOLE FIX: #6e6b67 measures 5.30:1 on `#ffffff` ✓ and 4.82:1 on `--surface-s1` #f6f4ef ✓, but only 4.02:1 on `--surface-s2` #e5e0d2 ✗ and 3.87:1 on the #dcdcdc badge fill ✗ — so muted ink on the shell ground and inside grey badges needs a darker step or a different surface, decided per surface rather than by one global darkening. INTERIM: none applied; Phase 8 is measurement only. Phase 9 unit 1. |
| 277 | TWO ADMIN SURFACES BOTH "SEND WORK BACK WITH NOTES" AND WRITE DIFFERENT STORES — the trap that made the Phase 8 harness report a view as unreachable nine times. `/admin/profile-update` → `sendBackProfileUpdate` writes `profileUpdate.status = "changes-requested"` plus per-field `adminNote`, reviewing POST-PUBLISH edits to a live profile. `/admin/review` → `submitReject` (`page.tsx:201`) is the ONLY writer of `changeRequests` outside the store, and it is the WIZARD's rejection path — it sets `status: "rejected"` and snapshots `baselineValue` from the live draft at that instant, which is standing rule 1's "moment of admin rejection". Nothing in either name distinguishes them. The harness driver was pointed at the first while the view it served (`/onboarding/submitted`) needed the second, and the only reason this surfaced as a clean "unreached" rather than nine screenshots of an unrejected profile is that the driver asserted on the END STATE (`changeRequests` non-empty) instead of on "the click landed". Recorded because the next person to automate either flow will reach for the wrong one. `/admin/review` also accepts `?focus=<stepId>`, which opens review mode with that field pre-flagged — a product-supported deep link, and a better driver entry than toggling a Radix checkbox whose only handle is an aria-label containing an em-dash. |
| 278 | A PLAYWRIGHT `addInitScript` SEEDER REVERTS ANY STATE THE APP WRITES, AND RETURNS GREEN WHILE DOING IT. An init script runs before the scripts of every document in the context — every navigation and every redirect hop, not just the first load. The Phase 8 seeder wrote the store payload unconditionally, so the `rejected` driver completed, asserted the app had written `changeRequests`, returned `ok: true, changeRequests: 1` — and then its own final `page.goto` re-ran the seeder, reset the store to the seed, and left the screenshot showing "In review" instead of the rejected dock. A passing result and a wrong picture, arriving from the seeder rather than the flow. FIX: seed once per context, guarded by a separate `somethings.harness-seeded` key the app never reads. For a seeded view this is a no-op (the payload is identical on every hop, so writing it once writes it the same); for a driven view it is the difference between evidence and fiction. Sibling of gap rows 228/266: tooling that fails silently in the direction of looking correct. |
| 279 | THE FOCUS SWEEP NOW RUNS ON A PRISTINE DOCUMENT — AND THE SUSPICION THAT PROMPTED IT WAS WRONG. `assert.mjs` ran `IN_PAGE` and then `focusNotObscured` against the same document, and the sweep itself focuses up to 60 elements in sequence, every one of which can scroll — so each element was measured against a document mutated by every element before it. A reload was added between them so each result depends on the view alone. THE STATED JUSTIFICATION DID NOT SURVIVE MEASUREMENT, and this row records the correction rather than the original claim. The trigger was D6 `/app/mentees` @landscape reporting 19 obscured elements in the full run against 2 from an ad-hoc direct call, which was read as contamination. It was not: the ad-hoc call skipped `IN_PAGE` and the reload, so it was a DIFFERENT sequence, not the same check twice. Run properly, the two full passes agree within 1% — `focusObscured` 654 before and 647 after, with near-identical breakdowns (`nav[bottom-nav]` 271 → 264, `div` 174 → 174, `a` 96 → 96, landscape 299 → 293). So 647 is a real, reproducible figure and is reported as one; the earlier decision to discard it was mistaken and was corrected in the same report. WHAT THE RELOAD DID change is per-view distribution (D6 @landscape 19 → 36) while leaving the total flat, because a reloaded page rehydrates app-evolved state rather than the seed. Reproducibility itself is verified the only way that means anything: one view run twice, byte-identical summaries. The lesson kept is narrower than the one first written — an ad-hoc probe that does not replicate the runner's sequence is not evidence about the runner. |
| 280 | `fullPage: true` SCREENSHOTS PUT FIXED CHROME IN THE MIDDLE OF THE PAGE, AND THE BASELINE IS HALF-READ BY EYE. A full-page capture makes a canvas as tall as the document and paints every `position: fixed` element at its viewport-relative offset inside it. MEASURED on `D3/xs.png` (`/app/activity` at 320): the four-item floating nav pill is painted across the activity list with eight more rows continuing below it, and the 40px dev bar below that. The page is correct; the capture mode invented the overlap. This matters more than a footnote because the screenshot matrix is the human-readable half of the Phase 8 baseline — a reviewer would file layout bugs that do not exist, and would simultaneously MISS the genuine overlap questions (SC 2.4.11 focus obstruction, nav-pill vs dev-bar z-order) because the evidence for those is precisely what full-page capture destroys. FIX: capture both. `<vp>.png` is the viewport — what a person at that size sees, fixed chrome where it really is, the shot to review for overlap; `<vp>-full.png` is the whole document, for reflow and down-page truncation. Neither alone is the baseline. Sibling of rows 278/279: tooling that fails in the direction of looking correct. |
| 281 | THE FLOATING NAV APPEARED TO COVER FOCUSED CONTROLS — THE REAL CAUSE WAS THAT THE PANEL NEVER SCROLLED (see row 283). MEASURED FIRST: 264 of 647 focus-obstruction hits attributed to `nav[data-slot="bottom-nav"]`, 293 of 647 at landscape (844x390), and on `/app/mentees` at 390px tall the nav pill occupying y=246-350 with the dev bar below it — 144px of a 390px viewport. Of the first 60 focusables, 29 landed in that band and all 29 stayed entirely covered; only 13 of 60 scrolled when focused. THE DIAGNOSIS WAS WRONG AND IS CORRECTED HERE. I concluded that `scroll-padding-bottom` (set at `app-shell.tsx`) was structurally unable to help, on the reasoning that scroll padding applies only when a scroll is performed and an element already inside the scrollport is "visible" to the browser. The premise was false: the panel was NOT the scroll container. `min-h-0` was missing on two flex levels, so the panel grew to content height and the DOCUMENT scrolled instead — which means `scroll-padding-bottom` was declared on an element that never scrolled, and was inert. Nothing was wrong with the token, the reserve, or the nav design. FIXED BY ROW 283, and the effect is not marginal: D6 @landscape 36 hits -> **0**, D3 @landscape 17 -> **1**, D6 @s and H2 @m at 0. The nav needs no height-threshold rule, no smaller footprint and no design change — the three candidate fixes proposed here were all answers to a misdiagnosis. LESSON: before concluding a CSS property cannot work, verify that the element it is declared on is doing the job you think it is. |
| 282 | RC-6 WAS MEASURING SVG CHILDREN AS CSS BOXES — the last of the four checker over-reporting classes, and the one its own output was labelling for me. An `<svg>` maps its children through `viewBox`, so a shape whose rect extends past the element's box is the coordinate system working, not content being clipped. The check counted those as lost content: 48 of 212 RC-6 hits, every one signed `svg.[object SVGAnimatedString]` — and that label was the tell, since `className` on an SVG element returns an `SVGAnimatedString` object rather than a string, so the signature could only ever have come from a namespace the check had no business in. FIXED by skipping any element whose `namespaceURI` is the SVG namespace, both as a candidate and as a child, so nested shapes drop out with the root rather than needing a tag blocklist. VERIFIED on A3 @xs: 3 hits → 2, the SVG signature gone, and the two survivors are real (40px and 20px lost on x, both `clipped`). CORRECTED FIGURE: RC-6 is **164**, not the 212 the committed baseline records — the baseline predates this fix and the next full pass will show it. Running order for the four classes, all found by distrusting a count larger than the number of plausible causes: touch-target `::after` (216), absolutely-positioned art (203), text under a truncating ancestor (384), SVG children (48). 1737 → 164. |
| 283 | `min-h-0` WAS MISSING ON TWO FLEX LEVELS, SO THE DOCUMENT SCROLLED AND THE RAIL SCROLLED WITH IT — user-reported, and the root cause behind the largest single a11y finding in the Phase 8 baseline. `AppShell` roots at `flex h-[calc(100dvh-var(--dev-bar-h,0px))]`, and the panel inside carries `h-full overflow-y-auto` so it should scroll internally while the rail holds. It did not. A flex item defaults to `min-height: auto`, which refuses to shrink below content height, so the intermediate column (`app-shell.tsx:122`) and `<main>` (`:133`) both grew to fit their content, overflowed the fixed-height root, and handed scrolling to the document. The panel's `h-full` then measured against an already-overgrown parent, so `overflow-y-auto` never engaged. MEASURED at 1440x900: `/app/mentees` document 1822px, a record 2539px, `/app/activity` 993px, and after a 600px scroll the 92px rail sat at `top: -600` — off-screen. Both elements already carried `min-w-0` for the horizontal axis; the vertical twin was simply absent, which is why it survived review — routes whose content fits the viewport (`/app/team`, `/app/settings`) behaved perfectly, so the defect was invisible except on tall pages. A SECOND, SUBTLER LEAK remained after the flex fix: 53px of document scroll on `/app/activity`, traced to a 1x1 `sr-only` paragraph at document y=953 with `documentElement.scrollHeight` reading exactly 953. An absolutely-positioned element resolves against the initial containing block when it has no positioned ancestor, so it escaped the scroller and extended the document — one invisible screen-reader label still dragging the rail out of view. FIXED by `min-h-0` on both flex levels plus `relative` on the panel to contain absolute descendants. VERIFIED: document height equals viewport height on all seven route/width combinations tested (320, 390, 1440, 2000), panel scrolling where content is tall, rail at `top: 0` after scrolling everywhere. KNOCK-ON: this is what gap row 281 was actually measuring — focus obstruction collapses (D6 @landscape 36 -> 0, D3 @landscape 17 -> 1) because `scroll-padding-bottom` finally applies to a container that scrolls. |
| 284 | STANDING RULE 6 IS RETIRED — mentees display their full name (product-owner decision, 2026-08-28). The rule read "Teens are FIRST NAME ONLY, everywhere, always. Never render a teen surname," was restated verbatim in every phase brief, and was enforced from `lib/record/teen-name.ts`. Reported as a regression ("all mentees should display their last name, it got removed at some point") and the report was accurate: `/app/mentees` did once list "Caleb Ortiz", "Elena Duarte", "Jonah Whitfield", because the pre-rule idiom `teen.goesBy ?? teen.name` fell back to the FULL name for the 23 of 26 teens with no nickname. Rule 6 replaced that with first-name-only; this reverses it deliberately rather than by accident. Row 254 records the enforcement. WHAT CHANGED, all at the chokepoint: `teenDisplayName` returns `teen.name`; `teenAvatarInitial` returns TWO letters instead of one (the single letter existed only so the surname initial stayed off screen, and a lone "C" beside a header reading "Caleb Ortiz" would now read as a bug). 47 call sites in 17 files, one function — the chokepoint was built against a sweep precisely so the naming decision would be one line whichever way it went, and that is what it bought. Verified over the fixture by an inverted test suite (211 passing) and in the browser: `/app/mentees`, `/app/people?view=mentees` and the record header all render full names, no first-name leftovers, zero console errors. ONE DELIBERATE LOSS: `goesBy` no longer wins, so the three teens with a nickname ("Pat", "Jordy") show it nowhere in a list, header or sentence — `goesBy ?? name` would have left exactly those three with no surname, which is the thing being fixed. The nickname survives on the record's "Goes by" row; `teen-name.ts` names the one line to change if preferred names should win again. ONE PLACE THE REVERSAL CANNOT REACH: `lib/activity/seed.ts` is a separate fixture universe in which EVERY person — teens and staff alike — is stored as a first name only, with no surname in the file. Giving the activity ledger surnames means authoring them for staff as well, or staff become the only people shown less fully than teens. A data decision, flagged rather than invented. ALSO OUTSTANDING: roughly eighteen comments across a dozen files still cite "standing rule 6" as live. The four that described current behaviour incorrectly were corrected (`people-demo.ts`, `subject.ts`, `roster.tsx`, `activity/seed.ts`); the rest explain why code does something it no longer does and need real editing, not substitution. |
| 285 | SC 2.5.8 TARGET SIZE — 584 failures were about a DOZEN primitives, and four copies of one of them. Cluster D carried 428 of 584 and H another 126, so the whole finding lived in two high-traffic clusters. FIXED, all with the Phase 0 `touchTarget()` helper (which until now had ZERO consumers — it was built in Phase 0 and `ChatAction`/`SegmentedControl` still apply the pattern inline): the table sort headers (132 hits across 9 labels), `NameCell`'s record link, and the billing-code chip. `/app/mentees` and `/app/team` now measure **0** target failures at 1280 and 390, from 47/viewport. FOUR TRAPS WORTH THE NEXT PERSON'S TIME. (1) THE SORT HEADER EXISTS FOUR TIMES as a byte-identical class string — `data-view.tsx:145`, `relationship-table.tsx:912` and `:954`, and `roster.tsx:514`. The first pass patched three, and `/app/mentees` still measured 20px, because the fourth renders it. (2) `20` WAS NOT IN `TOUCH_EXTEND` and the helper says not to round to a neighbour, so it was added with its arithmetic: 20 + 2×12 = 44, checked against the real geometry — a 20px button inside a 45px `<th>` with the first body row starting exactly at the cell's bottom edge, so the bleed cannot reach the row's overlay link. A header target that grew into the first row would make its top 12px sort instead of open. (3) `truncate` CLIPS ITS OWN `::after`. `overflow: hidden` means `touchTarget` on a truncating element silently does nothing — same class of trap as the template-literal class the helper file warns about. `NameCell` keeps `block` on the link and the ellipsis moved to an inner span. (4) `::after` DOES NOT WORK ON `<input>`; the search field was fixed by moving the pill's `py-1.5` onto the input so the input fills it (20px → 32px, pill unchanged at 34px) — `self-stretch` alone did nothing, because an input stretches to the parent's CONTENT box and the padding was on the parent. TWO FINDINGS THAT ARE NOT DEFECTS: the 27 `previous <date> →` hits are EXEMPT under SC 2.5.8's inline exception — the control sits in flowing text (`{lead}{" · "}<button>`) and its size is constrained by the line-height of non-target text; the checker does not implement that exception. And `Prototype scenarios+` (16 hits) is the dev bar, deliberately shipped in all environments. NOT A CRITERION ISSUE BUT WORSE THAN ONE: `/app/mentees` rows have no overlay link — `elementFromPoint` at 75% of a 61px row returned a plain `<td>` — so the only way into a record was a 20px strip of text. The extension fixes the criterion; a row-level overlay would fix the interaction, and is not done here. |
| 286 | THE CONTRAST TAIL WAS OPACITY MODIFIERS AND ONE UNPAIRED TOKEN — everything the `--muted-foreground` alias (row 276) did not reach. Measured empirically over 10 views × 2 viewports rather than reasoned about: 31 failing nodes reduced to 6 distinct causes, of which four are the phone-preview palette (row 287) and two were defects in the staff console. (1) THE `invited` STATUS BADGE WAS THE ONE ROW IN ITS MAP NOT USING ITS PAIR. `status-badge.tsx` styles every other status as a `--status-*-bg` / `--status-*-fg` couple; `invited` reached for the generic `bg-muted text-muted-foreground`, and since `--muted` IS `--status-neutral-bg` (#dcdcdc) that put muted ink on the neutral fill at 3.86:1 for 12px text — 6 nodes across the wizard. Fixed to `--status-neutral-fg` (#525252, 5.70:1), which the design system already supplied; the background is byte-identical, only the ink moved, and the row now reads like its four siblings. (2) OPACITY DILUTES A TOKEN PAST ITS OWN AUDIT. `text-muted-foreground/80` composites to #8b8985 = 3.49:1, and `placeholder:text-muted-foreground/70` to #9a9795 = **2.90:1** — the latter in `input.tsx`, `select.tsx` AND `textarea.tsx`, so every form field in the app had a failing placeholder. `--muted-foreground` was darkened precisely to clear AA and the modifier quietly gave it back. Opacity on an audited text token is a silent regression of that audit: the ratio in the token comment stops being the ratio on screen. THE DESIGN CONCERN IS ANSWERED BY MEASUREMENT, not waived — placeholder must stay visibly lighter than a filled value, and it does: placeholder #6e6b67 is 5.30:1 while a filled value (`text-foreground` #1f1e1d) is 16.64:1, so the two states remain obviously different. RULE WORTH KEEPING: never apply an opacity modifier to a text token that carries a measured ratio; pick a lighter token that has its own measurement, or leave it alone. |
| 287 | THE LAST 23 CONTRAST NODES ARE THE TEEN PHONE-PREVIEW PALETTE, AND FIXING THEM IS A DESIGN DECISION I HAVE NOT TAKEN. After rows 276 and 286, every remaining `color-contrast` failure across the sampled views is inside the preview of the teen-facing app — four pairs, all of them that product's own colours rather than staff-console styling: `#ffffff` on `--preview-accent` #b164ff at **3.42:1** (13px — chips, header, play control, 7 nodes across B8/A3/A4/G1/G2/E1); `#a5a5a5` on white at **2.46:1** (14px, the Spotify region, 12 nodes); `#c5c4c2` on white at **1.74:1** (12px, the vibe region); `#dbdbdb` on white at **1.38:1** (14px, Spotify). WHY THESE ARE NOT A SWEEP AWAY: the purple is brand identity, and the three greys are so light (down to 1.38:1) that they are either deliberate de-emphasis in the teen design or decorative elements axe is reading as text. Reaching AA on white would take `#a5a5a5` to roughly `#767676` — a visible change to somebody else's product surface. NOTE FOR WHOEVER PICKS THIS UP: the declared colour is NOT what axe reported. `getComputedStyle().color` returns the declared value while axe returns the COMPOSITED one, so a DOM scan for these hexes finds nothing — as it did here, twice. They are opacity-modified tokens (the same mechanism as row 286), so the fix is likely to be removing modifiers rather than editing hexes, and the search should be for `/NN` suffixes in the preview components, not for the values above. |
| 288 | THE RULE-6 COMMENT SWEEP, AND THE NICKNAME LOSS RECOVERED. Retiring rule 6 (row 284) left ~18 comments across a dozen files asserting a live rule, which is the same misleading-record problem the rest of this work has been removing — a future change reads them as constraints. Corrected, and the split between them turned out to matter: SOME SURFACES STILL SHOW FIRST NAMES ONLY, FOR A DATA REASON RATHER THAN A POLICY ONE. Everything in `lib/activity/` (`sentences.ts`, `inspector.ts`, `filters.ts`, `filter-dialog.tsx`, `activity-row.tsx`) still renders a first name because that module's fixtures carry no surnames for ANYONE, staff included. Those comments now say so instead of citing the rule, because the difference decides what a future fix touches: the fixture, not the renderer. THE ONE PLACE THE OLD COMMENT POINTED AT A REAL OPPORTUNITY: `roster.tsx` deliberately withheld the `goesBy` subtitle from teen rows, reasoning that the cell already showed the nickname AS the display name, so a "goes by" line would either repeat it or contrast it against a legal name the rule forbade showing. Both halves of that reasoning are now false — the cell shows the full legal name, so the subtitle contrasts rather than repeats. Restoring it recovers the single deliberate loss recorded in row 284: `goesBy` stopped winning in `teenDisplayName`, and without this the nickname rendered nowhere outside the record. VERIFIED on `/app/mentees`: full names ("Caleb Ortiz", "Imani Clarke", "Elena Duarte") and all three nicknames ("Pat", "Marc", "Jordy") on screen together, which is what the mentor rows have always done. No new copy — `NameCell` already carried both the subtitle and its wording. ALSO CORRECTED: `record-task-regions.tsx` warned that a surname in a composed sentence "reads as a case file rather than a person", and that judgement may still be right — but the instruction covered prose explicitly, so the note now records the consequence ("Caleb Ortiz finished 3 activities") and points at `firstNameOf`, still exported and no longer on the display path, if prose should ever diverge from lists. |
| 289 | THE ACTIVITY ROW LOST 61% OF ITS SENTENCE AT 320. MEASURED on `/app/activity`: the description box is 112px of a 320px viewport while the sentence needs 282px, so 18 of 46 characters survived — "You closed a risk f…", which loses both the subject and the object of the row's only content. 390px (the commonest phone width) gave 30 of 46; the full sentence only fitted from about 768. Not an SC failure — there is no horizontal scroll and reflow passes — which is exactly why it went unmeasured: a `truncate` that is working as instructed still destroys the content when the box is a third of the viewport. FIXED with `line-clamp-2` below `sm`, `truncate` above: 320 goes to ~36 of 46 characters, 390 and 430 show the whole sentence, and the row grows 44px → 60px on phone tiers only. WHY NOT THE OBVIOUS RESTRUCTURE: moving the timestamp to its own line would give the text the full width, but the dense row carries its action cluster ABSOLUTELY POSITIONED at the right edge with the time fading underneath it on hover, so a second flow line would run beneath that cluster. Two clamped lines cost one class and no structural risk; the dense desktop row is byte-identical above `sm`. FOUND ON THE WAY, PRE-EXISTING AND NOT FIXED: at 1280 the same sentence still clips, because the feed sits in an `xl:grid-cols-[minmax(0,40fr)_minmax(0,60fr)]` two-column layout whose left column is 276px — NARROWER than the same row at 768 (560px) — against 282px of content. It clips by about one character, so it is marginal rather than urgent, but it is the reason a wide viewport is not automatically the safe case: a two-column layout can give a component less room than a phone-sized one. |
| 290 | WHEN A TARGET WRAPS, `touchTarget()` IS THE WRONG FIX — padding is. The "Closed today" stat links on `/app/tasks` render 18px tall (72×18, 33×18, 46×18), which is the usual case for the Phase 0 helper, and applying it here would have introduced a worse bug than the one it fixed. MEASURED at 320: the cluster is a `flex row` that WRAPS — "1 escalation" and "1 flag" sit on line 1 (top 190, bottom 208) and "1 billing" drops to line 2 (top 212), leaving **4px** between the lines. A 13px `::after` bleed each way would have overlapped the two lines by roughly 22px, and a tap in the overlap belongs to both targets: the precise failure `lib/touch-target.ts` warns about in its "vertical only, deliberately" note, and unpredictable in a way a small target is not. At 1280 all three sit on one line, so the hazard exists only at the widths that need the fix most. THE DISTINCTION TO CARRY FORWARD: **padding participates in layout and a pseudo-element does not.** `touchTarget` is correct for a control with fixed neighbours and slack around it (a table header inside a 45px `<th>`, gap row 285); it is wrong for anything in a wrapping flow, because the extension cannot push its neighbours away. `py-1.5` here grows the box to 30px — clearing the 24px SC 2.5.8 floor — and the wrap gap grows with it, so adjacent lines touch and never overlap. VERIFIED at 320, 390 and 1280: heights 30/30/30, no overlapping lines, no horizontal scroll, and the only remaining target failure on the view is the deliberately-shipped dev bar. |
| 291 | `useState(() => Date.now())` IS SSR-UNSAFE, AND IT IS AN IDIOM IN SIX PLACES. The record Care tab was the ONLY view of 68 with a console error, and it produced one at all nine viewports: "A tree hydrated but some attributes of the server rendered HTML didn't match the client properties. This won't be patched up." React's own cause list names `Date.now()`, and its component trace pointed at `<CareTab>`. CAUSE: `care-tab.tsx:104` takes `now` from a `useState` initialiser, which runs on the SERVER as well as the client with a different answer each time, and nineteen things on the tab are derived from it. The intent behind it is good and worth keeping — "fixed at mount so a re-render can't shift every date underneath the reader mid-session" — it is only the placement that is wrong. FIXED with the `useMounted` gate standing rule 3 prescribes and this directory already uses (`active-hours.tsx`, `person-profile.tsx`): on the server and on the first client render `mounted` is false, both produce a quiet shell, so hydration matches by construction and the real tree renders on the next frame with the client's clock. The gate sits AFTER every hook — an early return above one would skip it. REJECTED ALTERNATIVE: seeding `now` from a module constant so both sides agree. That hydrates cleanly too, but then shows dates relative to a stale instant until an effect corrects them; a quiet shell for one frame beats a wrong date on a clinical record. VERIFIED: 0 console errors at 1280 and 390, 3852 characters of care content still rendering, other record tabs untouched, 211 tests passing. THE SAME LINE EXISTS IN FIVE MORE FILES — `admin/person/[id]/page.tsx:103`, `tasks-page.tsx:103`, `supervision.tsx:57`, `mentee-table.tsx:91`, `team-directory.tsx:86` — and none of them errors today. The likely reason is that their rendered strings are coarse ("3 days ago") so a few milliseconds never changes the text, while the Care tab renders something finer. That makes them latent rather than safe, and the fix for each is the three lines used here. Not swept in this unit, because five high-traffic surfaces changed speculatively is worse than five documented ones — but this is the same shape as the four-copies-of-a-sort-header problem in row 285, so it should not wait long. |
| 292 | THE PHONE PREVIEW WAS A SCROLLABLE REGION NO KEYBOARD COULD SCROLL. 26 `scrollable-region-focusable` violations across A3, A4, A4r, E1, G1, G2 and H8, concentrated at lg, xl and text200 where the preview content overflows furthest. MEASURED on A3 at 1280: 1252px of content in a 736px box, `tabIndex: -1`, and no focusable descendant — the preview is a deliberately non-interactive rendering of the teen-facing profile, so there was nothing to Tab to and the region could not be scrolled by keyboard at all. FIXED with `tabIndex={focused ? -1 : 0}` on the viewport in `mentor-profile-preview.tsx` — conditional because the region is only scrollable when it is NOT in focused mode; in focused mode it is `overflow-hidden` and a focus stop there would be a tab stop that does nothing. Verified 0 on A3, E1, G1 and H8, and H8 went completely clean. NO `aria-label`, deliberately: six ancestors deep there is no existing visible text to point `aria-labelledby` at, and inventing a string would be new copy. The rule requires FOCUSABILITY, not a name, and a plain div with `tabindex` takes no role that obliges one. If the region should announce itself, that needs a product decision about the words. |
| 293 | THE FOCUS CHECK WAS TESTING ELEMENTS BEHIND AN OPEN MODAL — 163 of 213 hits, and 154 from two views. `focusNotObscured` focuses candidates PROGRAMMATICALLY, and `el.focus()` does not respect a JS focus trap. Radix keeps a modal's background out of the tab order with a trap plus `aria-hidden`, not the `inert` attribute, so `.focus()` still lands there and the overlay then legitimately covers what was focused. `/hub` opens a modal on load, so C1 and C2 contributed 77 hits each — the checker focusing the hub's own buttons behind the scrim and reporting every one as an SC 2.4.11 failure. A keyboard user cannot reach any of them. FIXED by narrowing the SCOPE rather than filtering hits afterwards: while a modal is open the reachable set is the dialog's contents, so that is what gets tested. Verified C1 77 → 0 and B5 → 0, with D6 and H2 unchanged at 0. FIFTH over-reporting class in these checkers, after the sticky-ancestor rect comparison (288) and the three in RC-6 (touch-target `::after` 216, absolutely-positioned art 203, text under a truncating ancestor 384, plus SVG children 48). The recurring tell has been the same every time: a count far larger than the number of plausible distinct causes. |
| 294 | MUTED INK ON `--surface-s2` — THE SURFACE GAP ROW 276 PREDICTED AND COULD NOT FIX. Aliasing `--muted-foreground` to `--text-muted` cleared `--surface-card` (5.30:1) and `--surface-s1` (4.82:1) but left `--surface-s2` (#e5e0d2) at 4.01:1, under the floor for 12px text, and that prediction was recorded rather than left to be rediscovered. It surfaced on D10 `/app/tasks`: worklist rows sit on the shell canvas, and two spans per row — "to {mentorName}" and the mono age column — carried `--text-muted`. FIXED to `--text-secondary` (#58534b, 5.78:1 on s2), the design system's own next step, so nothing is invented and the hierarchy still reads (primary for the teen name, secondary for the metadata). D10 and D11 both went from 17 violations to **completely clean** at 1280 and 390. THE GENERAL RULE: `--text-muted` is safe on card and s1 and NOT on s2. Anything rendering muted ink directly on the app canvas needs `--text-secondary` instead — worth checking at review time, because the token name gives no hint that its safety depends on the surface underneath. |
| 295 | THE STEP GRADIENT MAKES MUTED TEXT FAIL OVER MOST OF ITS RANGE — reported, not fixed, because the fix is a design decision. B4 `/onboarding?step=examples` puts a "Back" button in `text-muted-foreground` over `STEP_GRADIENT` plus a decorative `<StepBackdrop />`, and axe measured `#6e6b67` on `#ff5001` at **1.61:1**. MEASURED AGAINST THE ACTUAL STOPS: muted ink passes at the gradient's 0% stop (#f3f9dc, 4.90 ✓) — which is where the top-left button actually sits — and fails at 50% (#ffdccc, 4.13 ✗) and severely on Brand/Tangelo (#ff5001, 1.62 ✗). So the button is probably legible in place and axe sampled a backdrop element behind it, but muted ink anywhere else on this gradient is not. AND `--text-secondary` DOES NOT RESCUE IT: 2.32:1 on the brand orange. Only `--text-primary` (#1f1e1d) clears every stop (5.07 on the orange). A SEPARATE DESIGN-SYSTEM FACT WORTH RECORDING: **white on Brand/Tangelo is 3.28:1** and fails AA for normal-size text, so the brand's primary colour cannot carry white body copy — dark ink is the only compliant foreground on it. Changing the Back button's resting colour to primary would collapse its `hover:text-foreground` affordance, which is why this is a design call rather than a token swap. |
| 296 | THE TARGET-SIZE TAIL — 48 real failures across six components, all fixed with PADDING rather than the extension helper. After the shared table primitives (row 285), what remained sorted into: the state-chip remove button in `record/organization.tsx` (18×18, from `p-0.5` around a 14px icon), `media-intro-field.tsx`'s "or upload a file" (80×16), `intro-examples-modal.tsx`'s trigger (132×16), `/admin/review`'s back link (20px tall), and `task-pane.tsx`'s "View chat context" (126×20). EVERY ONE TOOK `py-1.5`/`p-1.5` INSTEAD OF `touchTarget()`, for the reason established in row 290, and each for its own concrete geometry: the state chips are `flex flex-wrap` with `gap-1.5`, so a vertical bleed would overlap the chip row above; the back link has `mb-4` and content directly beneath, so an upward bleed would reach the header; and the examples trigger is a `DialogTrigger asChild`, which must stay a single prop-forwarding element (classes fine, wrapper fatal — the note in `lib/touch-target.ts`). VERIFIED at 1280 and 390: B10, H18, G1, D10 and D11 all measure **0** real target failures. WHAT IS LEFT IS DELIBERATE OR EXEMPT, not outstanding: 27 hits on `previous <date> →`, exempt under SC 2.5.8's inline exception (flowing text, size constrained by line-height of non-target text); 16 on the dev bar's scenario switcher, which ships in all environments by instruction; and one on `/dev/components`, the component showcase — the same class of dev-only chrome, and the shipping components it showcases use `<Button>` with `min-h-11`, which measures 44px. |
| 297 | RC-6 WAS MEASURING INSIDE DEVICE FRAMES — 132 of its 164 remaining hits, and the sixth over-reporting class in these checkers. The phone preview is a window onto taller content, and a rect comparison reads that window as content loss. Two distinct reasons, both properties of what a phone frame IS rather than of the code: (1) in focused mode the viewport is `overflow-hidden` DELIBERATELY, because the preview zooms to one region, and otherwise it scrolls, so nothing is unreachable; (2) THE STAGE INSIDE IS SCALED — `applyFocus()` sets a transform on `stageRef`, and a transformed child's rect legitimately exceeds its parent, which the check reported as 20–580px lost. A further 9 hits were `/dev/components` rendering the app shell inside a fixed 640px demo frame so the showcase stays scannable — the rail being cut off by that frame is the frame working. FIXED by opt-in attribute, `data-clip-viewport`, so the COMPONENT declares its own intent and `checks.mjs` carries no list of class names to recognise; a future frame gets the same treatment without touching the checker. VERIFIED: rc6 0 on A3, A4, B5, B6, B8, B9 and E1 — every preview-bearing view — and 3 on I1 before the dev frame was marked too. **164 → effectively 0.** RUNNING TALLY OF THIS CLASS OF ERROR, which is now the dominant theme of the whole verification effort: sticky-ancestor rect comparison (288 false), touch-target `::after` (216), absolutely-positioned art (203), text under a truncating ancestor (384), SVG children (48), elements behind an open modal (163), device frames (141). Every one was found by the same tell — a count far larger than the number of plausible distinct causes — and every one would have been reported to the product owner as real work if the number had been trusted. |
| 298 | THE CARE UPDATE BECAME THE SINGLE DOCUMENTATION ARTIFACT FOR EVERY PRESENTATION EVENT — and three separate things collapsed into it. (1) THE RETIRED PROMPTS: the care update's three Figma prose sections were "Where things stand", "Direction of care" and "What I am watching". Recorded here because the spec required the grep for them to come back empty, so they are quoted in this row and nowhere in `src/`. They were good clinical writing structure but not a documentation artifact: a presentation change needs a clinical summary, a diagnostic justification and a symptom trend, which is what replaced them. (2) THE PROVIDER NOTE IS RETIRED, AND "ZERO HITS" NEEDED A SCOPE. Two different things carried the name: the review card's `DecisionNote` INPUT (deleted, with its label and its gating), and `CareEvent.providerNote`, a generic timeline-detail field carrying text for presentation rows, decisions, flag resolutions, closures AND care updates. Deleting the data field would have gutted History expansion for three out-of-scope event classes, so the field survives and its doc comment now says what it actually is. `CareReviewDecision.note` — which WAS the second note object — is gone, replaced by `careUpdateId` pointing at the update that documents the decision. Reported at the Phase 0 gate; the scope decision was left to stand when the go came back without overriding it. (3) THE PENDING-APPROVAL CONCEPT FOR AD-HOC CHANGES IS GONE. An ad-hoc DSM-5 pick used to be synthesised into a `PresentationReview` (`pr-…-manual`) so it travelled the approve-with-note path — which put a supervisor's own change into a queue for that same supervisor to approve. A confirmed pick now goes straight to the care update form and committing applies the change immediately. VERIFIED end to end: F41.1 → F33.0 applied on commit, both entries appended and cross-referenced, no `in_review` anywhere in the care layer (the two remaining hits are the unrelated mentor onboarding lifecycle), and the system-raised cadence review task still raised, still rendered, still requiring action. |
| 299 | "ESCALATED" COLLIDES WITH "ESCALATION", AND THE COLLISION WAS KEPT BY DECISION. The care update's symptom assessment is Escalated · Maintained · Improved. `CareEventType` already carries `escalation` and `risk_flag`, and the record runs escalation workflows, so the same word now means two unrelated things on one page: a symptom trend, and a safety event. A reader scanning History sees both. REPORTED AT THE PHASE 0 GATE with "Worsened" recommended — it removes the ambiguity at no clinical cost, since nothing about the three-state control depends on the word. The product owner chose "Escalated" ("Go - Escalated"), which is a legitimate call if payer or billing vocabulary requires it. Recorded rather than silently accepted: the ambiguity is now a property of the vocabulary, and the next person to read a history row deserves to know it was deliberate rather than an oversight. Implemented as specified in `CARE_UPDATE_ASSESSMENTS`. |
| 300 | THE INFO CALLOUT DID NOT EXIST, AND THE THREE-STATE SELECT HAD NO CORRECT PRIMITIVE. Both were named by the spec as available; neither was. THE CALLOUT: nothing in `ui/` or `record/` rendered a tonal advisory block — the nearest precedents were inline status CHIPS, and a chip labels a thing rather than carrying a sentence. Built as `record/info-callout.tsx` on `--status-info-bg`/`--status-info-fg` (measured 6.19:1 light, 8.32:1 dark), with `role="note"` rather than a live region because the text is present from open and never changes. No left accent bar (a second signal for what the tone already says), no border or shadow (the house rules forbid nested cards and it always renders inside one), and the tint scoped to the callout rather than its container. THE SELECT: `record/segmented-control.tsx` offers `semantics="tabs"` and `semantics="group"` (role=group + aria-pressed, a mode toggle) — neither announces a required single-select in a form. `ui/radio-group.tsx` existed with ZERO consumers, so adopting it shifted no other screen, which the segmented control could not promise. VERIFIED: the group is ONE tab stop with arrow-key selection (index 1 → 2 on ArrowRight), which is the behaviour a radiogroup owes and a row of toggle buttons does not. |
| 301 | ABSENCE RENDERS EXPLICITLY ON THE CARE SUMMARY — AN INVERSION OF THIS PRODUCT'S OWN RULE, AND THE FEATURE. Everywhere else on the care record, absence renders nothing: no pattern block, no cadence row, no empty section. The evidence document does the opposite — every element that cannot be computed says so out loud ("No risk flags triggered this cycle", "None this cycle", "Insufficient data"). The reason is the reader's next action: they are about to assign a diagnostic code, and on a clinical record a MISSING LINE IS AMBIGUOUS between "none" and "not checked". Those two are opposite facts and the cheaper convention cannot distinguish them. Element 5 refuses under three contact days rather than hedging, because prose carries no visible confidence marker and a thin trajectory sentence reads exactly like a solid one. CONSEQUENCE FOR ANYONE TIDYING THIS LATER: a pass that removes "empty" states from this card to match the surrounding pattern would silently convert "we did not check" into "there was nothing", which is the one error this document exists to prevent. Cited in `lib/record/care-summary-doc.ts` and `components/record/care-summary-doc.tsx`. |
| 302 | THE BRIEF'S "TOP 1% / TOP 5%" THRESHOLDS ARE IMPLEMENTED AS TIER NAMES, NOT PERCENTILES. The concordance rule counts flags at or above a severity threshold, and the brief names that threshold by population percentile. `risk-taxonomy.ts` carries no percentiles and deliberately never has: A PERCENTILE ATTACHED TO A CHILD IS A RISK SCORE, and "a Top 1% teen" is the sentence that phrasing produces once it enters a UI or a log. The taxonomy names tiers by the RESPONSE they require instead. Mapping used, ordinal and unambiguous: top 1% → `imminent`, top 5% → `clinicalReview`, so `QUALIFYING_TIERS = ["imminent", "clinicalReview"]`. The rule's logic is unchanged — only the vocabulary — and the brief's own guardrail (the person never carries a tier) agrees with the substitution. Recorded so the mapping stays auditable without the phrasing entering the product. Cited in `lib/record/care-summary-doc.ts`. |
| 303 | PRE-FILLING THE DIAGNOSTIC STATEMENT FROM THE CARE SUMMARY IS EXPECTED, AND NOT BUILT ON PURPOSE. The care-update form's diagnostic field can arrive pre-filled; its only source is `review.rationale` — a rationale recorded WITH the recommendation, whose provenance the field states out loud ("Pre-filled from the recorded rationale"). Seeding it from the evidence document instead is the obvious next feature and is refused: the summary is what a supervisor READS in order to form a judgement, and the diagnostic statement IS that judgement. Wiring one into the other turns a clinical determination into an edit of generated text, and the record then holds a signed statement whose origin cannot be reconstructed — the author's name is on it, the words are not theirs, and nothing distinguishes a statement that was reasoned from one that was accepted. The two entries are CROSS-REFERENCED in History instead, which gives a reader both without merging them. Reported, not built. Cited at the pre-fill call site in `components/record/care-tab.tsx`. |
| 304 | THE CARE SUMMARY RAIL WAS REMOVED FOR WIDTH, AND TWO THINGS WERE LOST. The per-cycle evidence document replaced a collapsible right-hand rail and its tab-row launcher; the Care tab's grid is now one column. WHAT WENT: the persistent collapse affordance (a reader who wanted History at full width could get it on every record, and that preference — `careRailOpen`, store v34 — is deleted), and vertical compactness, since the page is longer with the document inline. WHY ANYWAY: element 5 states a three-part fragment as labelled `<dl>` rows, and those cannot be read at 320px inside a rail; and the rail had been squeezing History to roughly 60% width on every record, which was the more frequent cost. The `summaryState` prop went with it — it existed only to make the panel's loading and error states reachable for review, and a derived document has no async call to be slow or to fail. If a reader-controlled width preference is wanted back, it belongs to History as a table-level control, not to a rail that also decides where the evidence lives. |
| 305 | THE CARE SUMMARY HAS NO FROZEN/PREVIOUS-CYCLE STATE, AND CANNOT — THE SPEC AND THE WINDOW DEFINITION ARE MUTUALLY EXCLUSIVE. The brief asks for a draft/frozen lifecycle on the card, with a frozen summary naming its window so a reader cannot mistake a past cycle's evidence for a current read. That cannot be built as specified: the window is the interval SINCE THE LAST CODE UPDATE, and `presentation_reviewed` is a code event, so approving a review OPENS THE NEXT WINDOW IN THE SAME ACT THAT COMMITS THE OLD ONE. A card deriving from the current window therefore always shows the live cycle. This was found by driving the product's own Approve → care update → Save flow rather than by reading the code: the post-approval header rendered "Aug 28, 2026 – Aug 28, 2026 · previous cycle" — a zero-length window wearing a past-cycle label — and an earlier `frozen = review === null` guess had additionally been true for every record with no review pending, which is most of them. WHAT REPLACED IT: the committed cycle is a History entry of the new `care_summary` class, carrying its own frozen window, contact days, theme counts with denominator, per-tier marker counts and concordance call, cross-referenced to the care update written in the same act. So the evidence is not lost — it is where an append-only record keeps things. WHAT IS STILL MISSING: no way to view a PAST cycle's document, which is a real supervisor need ("compare this quarter to last") and needs a cycle selector plus a renderer for the reduced commit shape — the prose deliberately is not stored, so a past cycle can never render as a full document. Reported, not built. |
| 306 | THE THEME COUNT WAS DIMMED BELOW AA, AND THE CHECKER THAT SHOULD HAVE CAUGHT IT UNDER-REPORTED. `opacity-80` on the "7/9" fraction inside a neutral chip composited already-muted text to **3.71:1** against the chip background (axe, both widths, 18 nodes across four card states). Removed — and the dimming was backwards on its own terms: the label names the theme, the FRACTION is the checkable evidence, and it is the part a supervisor reads the element for. `tabular-nums` stays so a column of fractions aligns. THE METHODOLOGY FINDING IS THE MORE USEFUL HALF: a bespoke contrast scan over the same card reported 0 failures on 33 nodes at the same moment axe reported 18, because it read `color` and walked ancestors for a background but never read `opacity` — which composites text toward its background without touching `color`, and multiplies down the ancestor chain. So it measured the ratio the markup INTENDS rather than the one a reader gets. Fixed by accumulating opacity over every ancestor and compositing before the ratio; validated the only way that means anything, by restoring `opacity-80` and confirming the corrected scan fails on exactly the three nodes (3.74 vs axe's 3.71 — a rounding difference, not a disagreement). ADDS TO THE OVER-REPORTING CLASSES LOGGED IN PHASES 8–9 THE FIRST UNDER-REPORTING ONE: a silent pass is worse than a false alarm, because nothing prompts a second look. Rule for any future check: measure what is painted, never what is declared. | 
| 307 | THE TRACKER'S CARE VIEW (H8) IS SEEDED ON THE THINNEST POSSIBLE RECORD, SO THE POPULATED CARD HAD NEVER BEEN CHECKED. `SUBJECT.teen` is t21 — two contact days, one reading — where every element of the care summary refuses and the card renders no chips, no flag rows and no concordance call. That is a legitimate view to track and it is the reason row 306's violation survived a full green axe baseline: the failing nodes do not exist on t21. Verified here with a scoped runner over four states (populated t17, no-flags t19, thin t21, mentor tier) at two widths, axe-clean after the fix. NOT ADDED TO THE TRACKER MATRIX, deliberately — doing so mid-programme would move the baseline totals the last report was written against. WHAT SHOULD HAPPEN: H8's subject moves to a populated record and t21 is added as its own row, so both the populated and the refusing states are permanently covered rather than one of them being covered by accident. |
| 308 | THE `diverges` BRANCH OF THE CONCORDANCE RULE IS UNREACHABLE ON THE CURRENT ROSTER. Divergence needs three things at once inside one cycle window: conversation content available, two readings that DO NOT cross a severity band, and at least three flags carrying an `imminent` or `clinicalReview` category. No teen has the third — t17 is the only record with three flags in a window and two of them qualify, and its scores also moved a band, so it resolves to `concordant` twice over. Verified by deriving `careSummaryFor` across all 26 teens rather than by clicking through them. THE RULE ITSELF IS TESTED IN ALL THREE BRANCHES with real category ids from the taxonomy (`care-summary-doc.test.ts`), including the documented overlap resolving to `diverges` — so what is unreachable is the DEMO, not the logic. NOT MANUFACTURED, and that is the judgement call: making it reachable means authoring a teen with three imminent-or-clinical flags and deliberately flat scores, which is a consequential clinical fixture invented to fill a screenshot slot. Reported so the decision is visible. If it is wanted, the cheapest honest route is a new theme-fixture row on a teen whose flag log is extended in the same change, with the flat-score pair chosen from real band boundaries rather than tuned to fire the rule. |
| 309 | THE PHONE PREVIEW WAS UNREACHABLE ON A PHONE, IN TWO DIFFERENT WAYS, AND FIXING IT SURFACED TWO CONTAINER BUGS. (a) REACHABILITY. On the staff mentor record the preview rail rendered at every width and simply STACKED below the content below `lg` — present in the DOM, and in practice unreachable: a reader had to scroll the whole record to find the teen-facing card, with no control anywhere saying it existed. On the mentor's own editor the trigger existed but sat where the preview COLUMN sits at `lg`, which on a stacking layout is below every field in the section being edited. Both now carry a compact `PreviewTrigger` on the section heading's own row, right-aligned, 44px, `lg:hidden` so it can never appear beside a visible inline preview. (b) `DialogContent` IS `grid grid-rows-[minmax(0,1fr)]` — ONE explicit row — while `DialogBody` is written `min-h-0 flex-1 overflow-y-auto`, a FLEX child's declaration. So the HEADER took the `1fr` row and the body got an implicit `auto` one: the header stretched, the body never scrolled, and overflow was clipped. On a full-height side panel that rendered as a band of dead space under the title with the device frame cut off. The `side` variant supplies `flex flex-col`; THE BOTTOM SHEET HAS THE SAME LATENT MISMATCH AND IS LEFT ALONE, because it ships on six surfaces and changing their overlay's layout is not this change's business — recorded here instead. (c) BOTH BRANCHES OF `PreviewDisclosure` MOUNTED `children` AT ALL TIMES, justified on the grounds that only one is ever in the accessibility tree since the other's ancestor is `display:none`. True for a11y and not sufficient: `MentorProfilePreview` defines DOCUMENT-GLOBAL SVG ids for its silhouette (`bust`, `bustMaskMid`, `bustErodeCore`, …), so a second copy duplicates all eight and every `url(#…)`/`href="#…"` in it resolves to the FIRST copy's defs — with that copy hidden, a reference into a `display:none` subtree. Both copies render identically so NOTHING LOOKED WRONG; an id sweep is what found it. Fixed by mounting `children` in one branch at a time (and the same guard on the staff record's aside) rather than by giving the preview per-instance ids via `useId`: `CutoutSpray` in that same file deliberately renders its filters ONCE and documents duplicate global ids as an accepted tradeoff, and two conventions for one problem in one file is worse than not creating the duplicate. |
| 310 | TWO CONTRAST FAILURES INSIDE THE DEVICE MOCK, PRE-EXISTING, AND A SECOND BLIND SPOT IN THE BESPOKE SCAN. Axe on the open preview panel reports `color-contrast` on the fake iOS status-bar clock ("9:41", #ffffff on #b164ff, 3.42:1) and on the empty-draft Spotify placeholder ("On repeat", `text-foreground/40`, 2.46:1). NEITHER IS NEW: both reproduce at `xl` on the desktop rail, which this change did not touch — verified by running the same scan there rather than by assuming. They are properties of `MentorProfilePreview`, which DEPICTS a teen's phone: the clock is chrome of the simulated device rather than of this product's UI and should probably be `aria-hidden`, and the placeholder only appears when a draft has no track. Not fixed here — that component ships on six surfaces and the fix is a judgement about what a device mock owes a contrast checker, which deserves its own change. THE SCAN FINDING: a bespoke contrast pass over the same pages reported failures at a flat `ratio: 1.0` on avatar initials and tab labels — text whose surface is a CSS GRADIENT. `getComputedStyle(el).backgroundColor` is `transparent` for a `background-image` gradient, so the ancestor walk skipped the coloured surface and compared the text against white. Adds a second under-reporting class to row 306's opacity one, same rule: measure what is painted, never what is declared. Axe handles gradients and is the authority where the two disagree. **RESOLVED 2026-08-31 (commit `2594a64`), AND THE SCAN THAT FOUND IT WAS WRONG TWICE OVER.** Re-measured properly: the clock is fixed by a scrim (row 338, verified at 5.59:1 by decoding pixels), and the placeholder was NOT the ratio recorded here — it is `text-foreground/40` compositing to #999999 at 2.85:1 in light, and 1.27:1 in DARK, where `bg-card` went dark inside a mock whose frame was pinned white (row 337). Both fixed: the label takes `--text-muted` (5.30:1 on the card) because "On repeat" and "Hear from Jordan" are the only words in the row and the reader has to read them — the faded treatment belongs to the bar beneath, which is the actual placeholder. `aria-hidden` on the clock was the recommendation here and was NOT taken: the clock now shows the real time (it is checkable against the device in the reader's hand), so hiding it would hide true content. The mock now measures identically in both modes, which is what a light-locked device screen should do. |
| 311 | THE TASKS SCENARIO SWITCHER IS UNREACHABLE BELOW `md`. It is pinned bottom-right, and the fixed bottom nav (`md:hidden`, z-40) sits on top of it — Playwright reports the nav's subtree intercepting every click at the switcher's coordinates, which is how this was found rather than by looking. So on a phone the prototype's scenario control cannot be tapped at all. DEV-ONLY UI, so it is reported rather than fixed inside a revision about the top region: nothing a demo audience sees depends on it, and the fix is a placement decision (above the nav, inside it, or into the DEV bar that already hosts persona and teen scenarios) rather than a bug to patch. CONSEQUENCE FOR VERIFICATION, which is the part that matters: any harness driving scenarios has to do it at desktop width. The `tasks-top.mjs` runner therefore drives all four scenarios at `xl` and keeps the default at `xs` — the default carries a breach, so the "chips must not grow the row" claim is still exercised at 320px. A driver that silently failed to switch would otherwise report four identical scenarios as four passes, which is exactly what happened on the first run when the scenario was seeded into `localStorage` (it is `useState`, not store state). |
| 312 | SAGE AND ROSE BANDS ADDED FOR THE SEVERITY SCALE'S TWO ENDS. The band family was sand / amber / clay — routine, cautionary, escalation — with no "settled" reading and no acute one that is not clay's deliberate earthy orange ("earthy, not pink", by its own comment). B4 needs both: severity bands run mild → severe, and new/resolved content chips are an opposite pair. DRAWN FROM BLOOM RAMPS, not invented: sage is forest/50 on forest/700 (6.10:1), rose is blush/50 on blush/1000 (5.93:1). Dark had to be hand-mixed, exactly as the existing triad's dark values were — Bloom's ramps terminate in saturated colour rather than in a dark well, so there is no step that reads as a fill: sage `#374a30` (1.38:1 on the dark card) with forest/200 ink at 6.13:1, rose `#60393d` (1.36:1) with blush/200 at 6.76:1. Rose is pulled toward blue so it cannot be read as clay at the same luminance. CHOSEN TO LAND IN THE FAMILY'S 5.06–7.38 RANGE rather than merely clear 4.5 — a band that only scraped past would read thinner than its neighbours. NOTE THE PROPERTY THAT MATTERS FOR 1.4.1: every pale bg in this family sits within 1.25:1 of every other, so the bands are separated by HUE, not lightness. That is precisely why the label always travels with the tint and why no state anywhere is signalled by colour alone. The Figma source for the lozenge styles (Bloom node 3455:8395) could not be read — the Figma MCP server is unauthorized in this session — so these are derived from the ramp table in `bloom-design-system_1.md` and should be checked against that node when it is reachable. |
| 313 | SCORE-VS-CONTENT CONCORDANCE: UI REMOVED, MODEL KEPT. It was the summary's most consequential section — a deterministic rule comparing severity-band movement against qualifying risk markers, stating any gap in three labelled fragments a supervisor could check. WHY IT GOES: the "content" half came from the theme extraction, which is fixture data with no pipeline behind it, so the section presented a rule over two inputs when only one was real — and it refused for 23 of 26 records, because `contactDays` is 0 wherever the mock has no row. A refusal that frequent is not a designed empty state, it is a feature waiting for its data. WHAT IS UNTOUCHED: `concordanceFor` with its `reason` discriminator, `ConcordanceCall`, `commitFor`'s frozen `concordance` field, and all 20 of their tests. Committed summaries already in the record keep their call and History still renders it, so returning the section when a real extraction lands is a render rather than a rebuild. The dead style constants (`CHIP_NEUTRAL`, `RESULT_CHIP`, `RESULT_LABEL`) were deleted with it — a leftover style constant is an invitation to re-add a section without its rationale. |
| 314 | "Dr. Nissan" IN THE CARE FOOTER LINKS TO THE TEAM PAGE, NOT TO A PROFILE. A staff self-profile route does not exist in this prototype and building one is out of scope, so the footer's named decision-maker routes to Team — the surface where a reader can actually find that person today. Recorded so the destination is REPLACED rather than rediscovered: when staff profiles land, this href becomes the profile, and an anchored/filtered Team link is the intermediate step if Team grows the ability to focus one member. The link is deliberately not omitted in the meantime: a name that is the page's stated authority and cannot be looked up is worse than one extra hop. |
| 315 | THE SPARKLINE INHERITS A NARRATIVE-SHAPED GATE, SO IT IS SUPPRESSED ON RECORDS THAT HAVE SCORES TO PLOT. B2 asks for "assessment scores as dots on a time axis spanning the mentorship", and the strip draws from `carePatternFor`, whose thresholds were authored for the Care pattern CARD: minimum span, minimum event count, minimum number of event CLASSES, minimum assessments. The class threshold is the problem — it asks "is there a pattern worth a sentence?", which is the right question for a paragraph and the wrong one for a plot. MEASURED ACROSS THE FIVE DEV SCENARIOS: the strip renders on `steady` and `longitudinal` and is absent on `90-day review due` and `review overdue` (both t19, two comparable PHQ-9 readings and no flags) — records where two dots and a line would be exactly the useful thing. NOT FIXED HERE, deliberately: `carePatternFor` is tested and its thresholds encode a real editorial judgement about when a pattern is worth asserting. What the strip needs is its own selector — plot whenever there are two or more comparable assessments, regardless of class mix — which is a design decision about what the strip claims, not a mechanical loosening of someone else's threshold. Reported so the absence is understood as inherited rather than intended. **RESOLVED 2026-08-31 (commit `2594a64`).** `carePatternFor` takes a `gate` parameter defaulting to `"pattern"`, so every existing caller and test is byte-unchanged; the strip passes `"strip"`, which asks only for a non-zero span and two comparable assessments. `STRIP_MIN_ASSESSMENTS` is 2 rather than `SPARKLINE_MIN_POINTS`' 3 on purpose — three points is the floor for calling something a TREND in prose, and the strip calls it nothing; the trajectory sentence above it makes that claim under its own gate. ONE BUILDER, TWO GATES rather than a second function, because the geometry (lanes, months, score range, coalescing) is identical and two copies would drift on the first change to either. MEASURED AFTER: present on 4 of the 5 dev scenarios, up from 2, and absent only on day 1 — where there are no assessments at all, which is the correct absence. |
| 316 | HISTORY'S TWO-LINE CAP HAD TO BE ENFORCED ON BOTH LINES, AND ONE BREAK ONLY APPEARED AT THE WIDER VIEWPORT. B6 caps a collapsed row at two lines. Line 1 wrapped first: a long escalation title pushed the right-aligned type chip onto its own line, measured at 88 and 95px against a 70px two-line row. Fixing that left `steady`, `90-day` and `review overdue` still at 88px — and ONLY at 1280, because the two-column restructure makes the spine NARROWER at 1280 (688px) than at 1024 (836px, no rail), so "F33.1 — unchanged · Dr. Amara Patel · Supervisor" wrapped at the wider viewport and not at the narrower one. Both lines now hold one line with the flexible element truncating; every row in all five scenarios measures exactly 70px at both widths. THE LESSON WORTH KEEPING: on a layout with a rail, wider is not a superset of narrower, and a responsive check that walks from small to large in the usual order will miss defects that exist only above the breakpoint. Truncation is visual only — `text-overflow` leaves the full string in the DOM, so the accessible name is complete, and a `title` attribute gives pointer users the same thing. |
| 317 | THE LOAD-STATE AXIS IS GLOBAL BUT HAS ONE IMPLEMENTING SCREEN, AND THE CONTROL SAYS SO. `LoadState` was referenced in exactly one file before this change — `activity-ledger.tsx` — so ready / loading / error / partial exist on the Activity feed and nowhere else. Lifting the control into the dev bar makes it reachable from every screen, which raises the question of what it means on a screen that has no such states. TWO BAD ANSWERS AND ONE CHOSEN: hiding the control except on Activity would make a harness axis appear and disappear as you navigate, which is how a control gets mistrusted; leaving it unqualified would have it silently do nothing on four screens out of five, which is the version that wastes somebody's afternoon. So the label names its scope — "Load (Activity)" — and the scope comes from `LOAD_STATE_SCREENS`, so a screen that implements these states later is added in one place and appears in the label automatically. WHAT WOULD MAKE THIS ROW OBSOLETE: Tasks, the teen record and the staff record growing their own loading / error / partial branches, at which point the qualifier drops off on its own. |
| 318 | THE ACTIVITY ROW'S LINE 3 WAS RIGHT IN THE COMPONENT AND WRONG IN THE SEED, AND THE REPORTED CAUSE POINTED AT THE WRONG FILE. Reported as line 3 "bound to the wrong object — inheriting from the selected pane object or a single seed value". The second half was right: three bulk seed loops wrote `payload: { code: "F41.1", index: i }` for every event they generated, so a whole scenario rendered one billing code. The first half was not — `contextFieldsOf` reads each event's own payload faithfully, and anyone who started in the component would have found nothing wrong there. Fixed with `payloadFor(type, teenId, i)`, which varies by type AND by teen and is deterministic (indexed, not random) so a fixture can still be diffed. SEPARATELY REAL: the builder walked the whole payload as a chain of `if (field) push(...)`, so any event carrying a code got "Code under review F41.1" — survey rows, escalation rows and mentorship-pause rows included. It is now a `switch` on event type with NO default, so a new event type gets no line 3 until someone decides what its line 3 says, and the union cannot grow silently past it. Several types return nothing on purpose. |
| 319 | THE SEED CANARY WAS ON SCREEN, BECAUSE IT LIVED IN A RENDERED FIELD. The network scenario carried `reason: "clinical detail that must never reach network scope"` as bait for a broken degrading query — a good instinct in the wrong field, since line 3 renders `reason`. Rows read "Abuse disclosure · clinical detail that must never reach network scope" in a demo. Moved to `canary`, which nothing renders: a leak is still unmistakable in a payload dump or an assertion, and the row now reads like a record. THE PATTERN WORTH KEEPING: a canary belongs in a key with no renderer, and "is this field rendered?" is the question to ask before writing one. Swept the rest of the activity seed and copy layer for placeholder or instruction-like strings — none remain. Two payload strings are instruction-shaped and verified unrendered: `note` (never read by any renderer) and the `reason: "withheld"` sentinel, which the query explicitly filters. |
| 320 | THE POSSESSIVE ORPHAN AND THE "LITERAL SPACE" WERE ONE DEFECT, AND NEITHER WAS A SPACE. Reported separately as 'rows read "Maya 's conversation"' and as "'s conversation.' stranded on its own line". Measured: the text node after the teen chip begins `"'s conversation."` with NO leading space, and its first character rendered 204px to the LEFT of the chip's right edge — on the next line. A line break between a name and its possessive reads exactly like a space, which is why one defect was reported as two. The old code glued the chip to the word BEFORE it (stopping a chip from leading a wrapped line) and left the suffix free to break away behind it. Fixed by moving the first token after the name INSIDE the nowrap unit, so avatar + name + "'s" either fit or move down whole; taking the whole first token rather than matching `'s` also covers "Maya's." at a sentence end. BEFORE/AFTER, measured at 1280px on 13 detailed rows: last lines beginning with the possessive went **7 → 0** (`'s conversation.`, `'s billing`, `'s escalation.` ×2, `'s mentorship.`, `'s presentation re` ×2 → `billing`, `escalation.`, `mentorship.`, `presentation revie`, or the whole unit moved down). The wrap COUNT is unchanged at 11/13 and that is the correct outcome: these sentences genuinely need two lines at this column width, and the fix is about where the break falls, not how many there are. Chip line box 21.4px → 20px, exactly the declared `leading-[20px]`, so the baseline change removed a 1.4px inflation rather than adding one. |
| 321 | THE BILLING CODE NOW APPEARS TWICE ON A COMPLETED-REVIEW ROW, AND THAT IS A SPEC DECISION OVERRIDING A TESTED ONE. A test asserted the code must NOT appear on line 3 of a `BILLING_REVIEW_COMPLETE` row, with sound reasoning: "the sentence already ends with it; a context line repeating it reads as two different codes at a glance." Phase B1 specifies line 3 per event type explicitly, and for this type it is "code + status — F41.1 · Active". The status is the new information and it needs the code beside it to be readable, since "Active" alone would not say what is active. So the row now reads "…billing review — F41.1." on line 2 and "F41.1 · Active" on line 3. THE OLD CONCERN STILL STANDS and the better fix probably runs the other way: drop the code from the END OF LINE 2, not from line 3, because line 3 is where the status lives and line 2 already names the review. That is a change to `sentences.ts`, which phase B1 is explicit about not editing ("an event sentence is not this phase's to edit"), so it is reported rather than taken. The reversed test carries the same note, so nobody re-reverses it from the old comment alone. **RESOLVED 2026-08-31 (commit `2594a64`), AND THE FIX RAN THE OPPOSITE WAY TO THE ONE RECOMMENDED HERE.** This row recommended dropping the code from the end of line 2 in `sentences.ts`. That is wrong, and the reason is density: line 3 renders in DETAILED only, so the em-dash tail is the ONLY place the code appears in dense mode — removing it would have traded a duplication for a disappearance. So line 3 became the STATUS ALONE. "Active" is not thin in context: the code sits directly above it on the same row, so it reads as "that code is active", which is the sentence B1's table was reaching for. This diverges from that table's literal "code + status" for a reason the table did not account for. Both tests were rewritten to assert the property rather than the string — the code appears in the sentence and NOT in the context line — with the reasoning inline so nobody restores the duplication from the old comment. |
| 322 | THE BILLING TAB CARRIED TWO PROPERTIES AND ONLY ONE OF THEM IS A SCOPE. Removing it is right — the rail is a SCOPE axis and billing is a DOMAIN axis, so a tab could express only one combination and "billing on my caseload" was unreachable. But the view was `{ scope: "all", domains: ["billing"], types: [all four] }`, and that THIRD field is the part a naive removal loses: it admitted `action` and `attention` rows because, in its own words, "a code history is only complete if it includes the rows that opened and chased a review as well as the ones that completed it." `mine` and `network` admit narrower type sets, so filtering to billing on either would have hidden every opened and overdue row and returned a shorter ledger than the tab did — silently, since a shorter list looks like a working filter. SO THE PROPERTY MOVED TO THE FILTER: `admittedTypes` now admits the full type set whenever ANY domain filter is applied. It generalises honestly rather than as a billing special case — "show me risk flags" omitting the raised and overdue rows would be the same defect — and it only widens on an explicit narrowing, so the default views still read as records rather than queues. |
| 323 | A4's FILTER-RESET RULE IS REVERSED, AND IT WAS CORRECT UNTIL THE RAIL STOPPED MIXING AXES. It read: "switching views resets filters. A view is a different question, and the filters were answers to the previous one." True while the rail held a Billing tab — carrying "only escalations" into a domain ledger really did answer a question nobody asked. With the rail purely scope and the filters purely domain the two are ORTHOGONAL, and clearing one when the other changes destroys the combination the instant you toggle, which is the entire reason the tab was removed. WHAT STILL CLEARS, because it stops applying rather than because a view changed: `staffId` (narrowing to one colleague is meaningless when the scope excludes colleagues, and leaving it set would be a filter with nothing to filter, silently returning nothing) and `unreadOnly` (a question about one view's unread set, which the rail already shows per tab). Verified: a Billing chip survives a Team → My caseload switch. |
| 324 | THE 30-DAY FIXTURE'S DAY DIVIDERS FIRED ON 42 OF 54 ROWS, AND THE FIX WAS ALMOST REVERTED BY THE WRONG RULER. Events were placed at `ago(floor(i * 48 / 54), …)`, spreading 54 events nearly evenly: measured by the feed's own `dayKeyOf`, 48 populated days of which **42 held exactly one event**. Grouping that never groups is chrome, and it also meant A6's coalescing was only ever visible in the heavy-day scenario. Replaced with an explicit `[daysAgo, count]` plan — 12 populated days, 3–8 events each, gaps of one to nine days, spanning 48 days so two populated days sit beyond the window and "Load older" still reveals real rows. THE MEASUREMENT LESSON IS THE REUSABLE PART: the first metric grouped by `toISOString().slice(0,10)` (UTC day) while the feed groups by local day, and it reported the corrected fixture as 22 days with a max of 5 instead of 12 days with a max of 8 — a passing change looking broken. A second, real bug hid inside that noise and was found separately: the within-day spread used `1 + k * 2.5` hours, and since `SIM_NOW` is 01:40Z any offset over ~1.6h lands on the PREVIOUS calendar day, splitting each planned day in two. Rule: when a metric and the product disagree about what a day is, the metric is wrong — measure with the product's own key. |
| 325 | THREE TESTS ASKED FOR A VIEW THAT NO LONGER EXISTS AND `viewById` ANSWERED ANYWAY. They passed `viewId: "billing"`, and that helper falls back to the default view rather than throwing — so after the tab was removed they silently began querying My caseload. ONE OF THEM CAUGHT IT and only by luck: "out-of-caseload rows stay code-free" asserts there ARE degraded rows, and `mine` scope has none by definition, so it failed loudly. The other two would have kept passing against the wrong question forever. Rewritten as `billingLedger()` — widest scope plus the billing domain filter, which is B2's actual contract — so the tests now assert the replacement rather than a fallback. WORTH CONSIDERING SEPARATELY: `viewById`'s silent fallback is friendly for a URL parameter and hostile for a test. A `viewByIdStrict` that throws on an unknown id, used from tests, would have turned this into a compile-or-fail rather than a coin flip. |
| 326 | THE BLOOM LOZENGE NODE'S OWN SOFT VARIANT FAILS AA ON FOUR OF ELEVEN HUES, INCLUDING BOTH THIS FEATURE NEEDS. Read from Figma node 3455:8395 once the server was authorised, and MEASURED off the render rather than inferred from the variable list: the soft variant is `X/100` fill with `X/700` ink. Sampled pairings — emerald 3.55:1, blush 2.81:1, lime 3.74:1, violet 4.27:1 FAIL; sky 6.19, fuchsia 6.80, neutralGrey 5.70, ecru 5.22, periwinkle 5.20, apricot 5.07, ivory 4.71 PASS. B4 asks for BOTH the Bloom lozenge styles AND 4.5:1 on every pairing, and for green and rose those cannot both hold. RESOLUTION, minimum deviation: the FILL is Bloom's exactly — it is what carries hue identity at a glance — and only the INK moves, to the next Bloom step that passes. sage `emerald/100 #beefd6` × `forest/700 #406333` = 5.41:1; rose `blush/100 #ffd9df` × `blush/1000 #b70b1f` = 5.27:1. Every value is a real Bloom token; nothing is invented. THE GREEN ALSO CHANGED RAMP, which is a straight correction: the first version used `forest`, from `bloom-design-system_1.md`, and the lozenge node uses `emerald` — a ramp that document does not contain at all. So the earlier fill was simply the wrong green. The ink stays forest/700 because emerald's darker steps are not exposed by the node, and a real token from a neighbouring green ramp beats an invented emerald. Both fills sit inside the existing sand/amber/clay family's paleness range (1.27 and 1.29 against the white card, vs the trio's 1.15–1.40). The node is LIGHT-ONLY, so the dark values remain derived — but dark sage moved into emerald's hue (150°) so the two modes are one colour rather than two different greens. |
| 327 | THE MO-4.1 HISTORY REFERENCE SHOWS A NEUTRAL SCORE LOZENGE; B4 SPECIFIES A TINTED ONE. WHAT THE REFERENCE DID CORRECT: the Care summary trigger is a FILLED dark pill (`#1f1e1d`, white text, sparkle), not the outline pill first built — fixed.
