| 328 | THE ACTIVITY PANE HIDES FLAGGED CONTENT WHERE THE TASKS PANE SHOWS IT, AND THAT DIVERGENCE IS THE POINT. `/app/tasks` renders a teen's flagged message the moment its pane opens, and that is correct there: Tasks is an active queue of items a supervisor must act on now, the content IS the thing being judged, and one item is open at a time. Activity is a browsable 30-day history, so identical content has a far wider exposure surface — a reader scrolling a month of rows would surface teen messages incidentally, dozens of times, while looking for something else. So the Activity pane shows the decision, the category and the reviewer notes by default and puts the MESSAGE behind an explicit "View flagged content" control, with a line saying why ("A teen's own message. Opening it is recorded."). REVEALING LOGS, through a second seam added beside `recordNetworkAccess`: `recordFlaggedContentAccess`, same no-op-with-the-call-site-in-place discipline, named per event rather than per render because this is always a deliberate act on one row. `flaggedContentFor` is the ONLY path to the content, so there is no way to read it that bypasses the log. THE DISCLOSURE RESETS PER SELECTION — the pane is keyed on the row id, because otherwise one reveal would open every subsequent flagged message for free; verified by revealing on one flag row and confirming the quote is absent after selecting a different one. SOMEBODY WILL EVENTUALLY MAKE THE TWO PANES MATCH. This row and the comments in `evidence.ts` and `event-evidence-pane.tsx` are the reason not to. |
| 329 | A12'S OBJECT INSPECTOR IS SUPERSEDED, AND ITS REASONING WAS THE PART THAT WAS WRONG. A12 specified a four-field summary of the row's OBJECT, reasoning that a ledger row has no body behind it. Every event in this feed acted on an artifact, and the artifact IS the body: a billing review has a code change and a review document, a survey has a score and a trend, a closed flag has a decision and the content it was about. So the pane now answers "what was decided, on what basis" and the record — one click away from every row, and from the pane's own persistent footer link — answers "who is this teen". `object-inspector.tsx` is deleted; `objectDetailFor` remains in `inspector.ts` because `DEGRADED_MESSAGE` and the teen/staff name helpers are still used, and deleting a tested module for tidiness is not this phase's business. THREE TYPES OPEN NO PANE AND THAT IS A FEATURE: mentorship paused/matched carry a reason and two names that the row's own third line already renders, and a presentation review's sentence already carries its code and outcome — a pane that restates the row it was opened from has not earned a column. The opened/overdue variants join them: the pane is about what was DECIDED, and "pending" is not a decision. `NO_PANE_TYPES` is an explicit list and a new event type falls through to no pane, so adding one is a decision rather than an omission. |
| 330 | THREE OF BLOOM'S SIX TYPE ROLES CANNOT BE BUILT HERE: ONLY TWO FONT CUTS ARE LOADED. `layout.tsx` self-hosts exactly TWK Lausanne 400 and Avantt Bold 700 — `src/app/fonts/` holds four files and no others. Bloom's ramp needs, and this app cannot render: **Lausanne 700** (Label/Medium, Label/Small and Body/Large-Bold — note Bloom's small labels are BOLD, not medium), **Avantt Regular** (Title/Large-Regular), and **Geist Mono** for the entire Label-Alt role, which Bloom reserves for timestamps, codes and data — exactly what the care record's score lozenges and billing codes are. THE DANGEROUS PART IS THAT REQUESTING AN ABSENT WEIGHT DOES NOT FAIL: `--type-label-sm` and `--type-field-label` both declared `500`, a weight with no file behind it, so the browser was SYNTHESISING it and the tokens read like a considered decision. Both are now `400`, the weight that actually renders. WHAT SHIPPING THE CUTS WOULD UNLOCK: Lausanne 700 alone fixes both label stops and Body/Large-Bold, which is most of the shortfall; Geist Mono would let codes and timestamps take the role Bloom designed for them, and `--font-mono` currently falls through to a system stack. Until then the ramp is implemented in two faces, and every token that had to compromise says so in its own comment. |
| 331 | A BLANKET `tracking-tight` ON EVERY HEADING MADE BLOOM'S TRACKING RULE UNIMPLEMENTABLE. `globals.css` carried `h1, h2, h3 { @apply font-display tracking-tight; }` — −0.025em on every heading, which resolves to −0.4px at 16px. Bloom specifies tracking PER ROLE: −2% Display, −1% Headline, 0 for Title, Body and Label. So a Title-level `h2` carrying `--type-card-title` measured at −0.4px when the ramp says 0, and every explicit `--type-*-tracking` was fighting an inherited value rather than setting one — which is why the old tokens carried hand-picked pixel trackings (−0.3, −0.4, −0.16, +0.3, +0.2) corresponding to no rule at all. The blanket tracking is removed and the face is kept; tracking now belongs to the stop, which is where Bloom puts it, and the percentages are resolved at each size (−1% of 32px = −0.32px). Verified: card titles and section headings now compute `letter-spacing: normal`. |
| 332 | THE CARE SPARKLINE'S MARKS FAIL SC 2.5.8 BY OBSCURING EACH OTHER, AND IT PREDATES THE TYPE WORK. Axe reports six `target-size` violations on the summary card: the mark buttons are 24×24 but sit on a shared time axis, so adjacent marks overlap and the UNOBSCURED area drops to 23×24, 24×20 and — worst case — 4.9×24. VERIFIED PRE-EXISTING, not a consequence of Phase 7: the same six violations reproduce identically on the tree at `3caf0f3`, and mark geometry depends on `xPct` and the rail width, not on any type token. It arrived with `care-tab-01`, when the strip moved from the wider Care pattern card into the 380px rail. NOT FIXED HERE, and the options are all real tradeoffs: spreading marks to guarantee 24px separation would put a mark at a date it did not happen, which is not acceptable on a clinical strip; a taller or wider strip is not available in a rail; shrinking the targets fails harder. The defensible route is SC 2.5.8's EQUIVALENT exception — every mark's event is reachable from History, where each row is a ≥44px target with a date gutter, so no information or action is exclusive to the undersized target — but the honest version of that argument needs the actions to match, and a mark JUMPS to an entry where a row EXPANDS one. The cheapest true fix is probably to generalise the existing same-day flag coalescing from "same day" to "within one target width", which is the constraint that actually binds; that needs a span-relative threshold in `care-pattern.ts` and its tests. Reported. |
| 333 | ROW 332 IS RESOLVED, BY DROPPING AN AFFORDANCE RATHER THAN BY GEOMETRY. Six `target-size` violations on the sparkline, worst case 4.9×24 against SC 2.5.8's 24×24. Fixed in three measured steps. (1) EDGE CLIPPING was most of it, not mark overlap: a 24px button centred on a point at 97% put half its box outside the strip. The data range now maps into an inset band (`EDGE_PCT = 4`), which is a uniform linear transform — dates unchanged, relative spacing identical. 4.9px → 21.1px. (2) CROWDING: marks closer together than one target width now merge, per lane and only when their plotted y is also within 20px, with a label naming a COUNT AND A RANGE ("2 flags · Aug 25 – Aug 28") because a merged mark cannot claim either date. This generalises the data layer's existing same-day flag coalescing from "same day" to "closer than one target" — the same editorial judgement, measured in pixels because that is the constraint that binds. Verified conservative: t17 keeps separate marks and merges only two flags three days apart; t18's four spread assessments stay separate. 6 → 2. (3) THE ARITHMETIC SAID THE REST WAS UNREACHABLE: the assessment lane is a RANGE, so its boxes sweep the band ±12px. Three interactive lanes need **94px** — a 47% deviation from the brief's ~64 — while TWO need **70px**, a 6px deviation, with zero overlap. So the presentation ticks lose their hit target and keep their mark: there are typically one to three, a presentation change is the least likely jump target on the strip, and each is already a full-width History row. The tick is still drawn, so the third class stays visible. Axe CLEAN. |
| 334 | PHASE 10 AUDIT — TWO MEASURED FINDINGS, ONE FIXED AND ONE REPORTED. (a) MEASURE: the Tasks pane's AI rationale ran to 100 characters per line at 1440px against the 45–75 convention, because the pane is 60% of a wide viewport and the paragraph had no constraint. `max-w-prose` on `SystemBlock` brings it to ~78 average-glyph characters, which is roughly 65 in `ch` terms — inside the range. Note the metric: characters-per-line measured from average lowercase advance over-counts relative to `ch`, so the care record's prose at "78" was already compliant and was left alone. (b) SPACING IS 31–40% OFF THE 4px SCALE, and this is reported rather than swept. Measured over every applied margin, padding and gap inside `main`: care 35% off-scale across 15 distinct values, tasks 40% across 12, activity 31% across 12. The offenders are Tailwind's half-steps — 2px (`0.5`), 6px (`1.5`), 10px (`2.5`), 14px (`3.5`) — which ARE on Tailwind's scale and are NOT on an 8px-base-with-4px-half-step system. NOT FIXED: normalising a third of every gap in the app is a large, high-risk change for a benefit no reader would name, and it would churn layouts verified this session (the History two-line cap, the 44px tab row, the panel geometry). The bounded action is to stop adding half-steps in new work and normalise opportunistically when a component is next touched anyway. |
| 335 | THE AI TAG MOVED OFF THE CARD AND INTO THE POPOVER, WHICH WEAKENS THE READING OF GUARDRAIL 6 THAT ROW 302 ESTABLISHED. `Provenance` rendered an always-visible "✨ AI" pill beside an info icon, on the argument that attribution on a clinical record must be unskippable. Product owner's call, 2026-08-31: the pill repeated on every generated section of one card — five call sites on the care summary, two of them rendering simultaneously on t13 — and read as clutter. The icon now carries the affordance alone and the tag sits at the top of the panel it opens, above the note, because the note explains what "AI" means here and reading them the other way asks the reader to hold an explanation before knowing what it explains. WHAT THIS COSTS, STATED PLAINLY: attribution is one interaction away rather than visible at rest. The icon is visible; the WORD is not. That is a real reduction against "attribution visible without scrolling", and it is a deliberate trade of unskippability for legibility, not an oversight. What did NOT change: the tag is unconditional once the panel is open, there is still no prop to suppress it, and a generated block cannot render without one. THE NOTE ALSO LOST ITS OWN PREFIX. `PROVENANCE_NOTE` opened "AI-generated from assessment history…", so the panel read "AI" directly above "AI-generated" — the reader told twice in eleven characters. It now opens "Generated from…" and the tag carries the attribution. `care-summary-copy.test.ts` was widened rather than relaxed: it asserts the sources sentence, the refusal ("not itself a clinical determination") AND `label = "AI"`, so a later copy pass cannot quietly delete the tag as well and leave the surface with no attribution at all. | 
| 336 | A HOVER-AND-CLICK DISCLOSURE NEEDS A PIN MODEL, AND THE SYSTEM HAS NO CONVENTION FOR ONE. `Provenance` opened on hover, focus and click, with the click wired as `setOpen(v => !v)`. For a mouse user that made the icon look broken: the pointer arriving fires `mouseenter`, which OPENS the panel, so the click a moment later closed the very thing being clicked — and it stayed closed until the pointer left and came back. FOUND BY DRIVING IT, NOT BY READING IT. A Playwright `click()` is a real hover followed by a real press, which is exactly the sequence a person performs and exactly the half the toggle logic had not modelled; the harness call timed out waiting for a panel that had opened and shut again inside 200ms. Reading the component would not have surfaced it, because each handler is correct alone. A click now PINS: the panel survives the pointer leaving, and a second click unpins. Escape and an outside mousedown clear the pin. The pin is a `useRef`, not state — nothing renders differently when it is set, and the first version used `setPinned` with an updater purely to READ the current value inside a handler, which is a setState-as-getter smell and drew an unused-variable warning that was the lint telling the truth. THE GENERAL RULE, worth a system convention: any disclosure that opens on hover AND accepts a click needs three states, not two — closed, open-by-hover, pinned — because open-by-hover must not be the state a click toggles against. 1.4.13's hoverable/dismissible/persistent requirements are satisfied by the same machinery. | 
| 337 | `.theme-light` — A SUBTREE THAT DOES NOT FOLLOW THE READER'S THEME, WHICH THE SYSTEM HAD NO WAY TO EXPRESS. The phone mock in `mentor-profile-preview.tsx` pinned its own frame with `bg-white text-[#1f1e1d]` but let everything inside keep theme-following tokens, so in dark mode the device screen became a white page carrying dark `bg-card` panels and dark ink: "On repeat" at 1.27:1, three section labels at 2.82:1, six failures where light mode had three. A device screen is a photograph of another product, not a surface of this one — it does not have a dark mode because the reader's OS setting is not the simulated phone's. `.dark` sets its tokens on the ROOT and custom properties inherit, so re-declaring the light values on any element gives that element and its descendants the light palette whatever the app theme is. Every `:root` block that declares a colour token now carries `.theme-light` as a co-selector, in both token files. COVERAGE IS MEASURED, NOT ASSUMED: 179 tokens are declared in light and 179 are overridden in `.dark`, and the only two the dark side declares alone are `--breakpoint-md` and `--breakpoint-aside`, which are lengths — so no colour token can leak a dark value into a locked subtree. CHOSEN OVER PATCHING THE FIVE FAILING CALL SITES, which would have left the next child added to the mock to rediscover the same bug. THERE IS NO `.theme-dark` COUNTERPART: `.dark` already is one, and a second spelling would be a fork. Reusable for any always-light surface — a print preview, an email preview, a second device mock. Recommend promoting it to the design system as the named scoping primitive for "depicts another product's UI". | 
| 338 | TEXT ON A BRAND-COLOUR GROUND HAS NO CONVENTION, AND THE ONE CASE HERE COULD ONLY BE FIXED BY MOVING THE BACKGROUND. The mock's status-bar clock is #ffffff on the hero's #b164ff — 3.42:1, and white is already the BEST ink available against that purple (black measures 6.13:1 but is wrong for a status bar over a saturated ground, and the strip also sits over a photo in some states). So the background moved: a scrim. THE ALPHA IS UNIFORM WHERE THE TEXT IS, THEN FADES, and that is the part worth keeping. A plain `to-transparent` gradient puts the text in an INTERPOLATED alpha, making the composite a function of the strip's padding — measurable only by sampling pixels and silently wrong the next time `pt-3.5` changes. Holding rgba(0,0,0,0.25) flat through 44px (the text band is y≈14–31px) then fading to 80px makes it exact arithmetic: rgb(133,75,191), and white on that is 5.59:1. 20% was the first value and measures 4.85:1 — passing but thin. VERIFIED BY DECODING THE SCREENSHOT, because no computed-style checker can see it: the scrim is a gradient on a SIBLING span, and `bgOf` walks ancestor background-COLOURS. `scripts/responsive/status-bar-pixels.mjs` inflates the PNG itself with `node:zlib` — parse IHDR, inflate the IDATs, undo the per-scanline filter — and samples the glyph core and the modal ground. It samples EXTREMES, not percentiles: a 13px glyph at 1× is mostly antialiased edge, and a 2nd-percentile pick landed on a half-covered pixel. AND IT CAUGHT A BUG REASONING HAD NOT: the scrim was painting ON TOP of the clock. The clock and the glyph row are STATIC elements and a positioned sibling paints above static ones whatever the DOM order, so the "antialiased" rgb(204,204,204) first measured was 255 × 0.8 — white text seen through its own scrim. `-z-10` puts it under; the status bar's own `z-[15]` on a positioned element opens the stacking context that keeps it inside. | 
| 339 | A THIRD UNDER-REPORTING CLASS IN THE BESPOKE CONTRAST SCAN, AFTER ROW 306's OPACITY AND ROW 310's GRADIENTS: THE ELEMENT'S OWN BACKGROUND. The ancestor walk started at `el.parentElement`, but a button carries `bg-primary text-white` on ONE element, so its text sits on that element's own background. Starting at the parent measured white-on-primary as white-on-page and reported a phantom `1:1` — three of them, on "Submit for review" and the mobile bar's "Mentor", which looked exactly like catastrophic failures and were not failures at all. Two of five reported failures on the preview page were this bug. Fixed by starting the walk at the element itself. SAME RULE AS 306 AND 310, now stated as a habit rather than a finding: VALIDATE THE CHECKER AGAINST A CASE WHOSE ANSWER YOU ALREADY KNOW, every time — a checker that under-reports is worse than no checker, because its silence is read as a pass. The scan also now buckets gradient-backed text as "unmeasurable" rather than "failing", and tags each hit with whether it sits inside `.theme-light`, without which "the mock is broken" and "something else on the page shows the same string" are indistinguishable — and the page does render those labels twice. | 
| 340 | THE ILLUSTRATION PALETTE IS BEING USED AS INK, AND NOTHING IN THE SYSTEM SAYS IT MUST NOT BE. `--doodle-green` (#3dbe6b) set the record overview's "Bonus: $150" at 14px semibold on the white card: 2.39:1, and after this session's other fixes it was the ONLY axe violation left standing on that surface, at every one of nine viewports. The doodle hues (`--doodle-tangelo/yellow/green/sky/purple/pink`) are tuned for hand-drawn artwork on a warm ground, where nothing depends on reading them, and they are declared as raw hexes that do not follow dark mode. `--status-positive-fg` (emerald/700 #0f7542, 4.53:1) is the token that already exists for "a good number, in words", and it is mode-aware. The BAR below the figure keeps the doodle green: a filled track against `--surface-s1` beside a `progressbar` that states its own value in `aria-valuenow` is decoration, not a meaningful graphic carrying the only copy of the information. Recommend the design system state the rule out loud — illustration hues are never text or UI-boundary colours — since the palette's own naming does not imply it. | 
| 341 | AN ABSOLUTELY POSITIONED TOOLTIP INSIDE A SCROLL CONTAINER GIVES IT A PERMANENT SCROLLBAR, AND `overflow-x: clip` DOES NOT FIX IT. The left rail carried a horizontal scrollbar on every console route at every width ≥ md. Cause: the AccountPopover's INVISIBLE "Account" hover tooltip, positioned `left-full ml-2`, 58px wide, extending 48px past the rail's right edge — and an absolutely positioned descendant counts toward its scroll container's scrollable overflow whether or not it is painted. The rail carries `overflow-y-auto`, and per CSS Overflow a non-`visible` value on one axis forces the other off `visible`, so `overflow-x` computed to `auto`. Measured: client 92px, scroll 140px, scrolls by 48px. THREE PLATFORM FACTS WORTH KEEPING, all measured on the live rail rather than reasoned about. (a) `scrollWidth` is NOT a test for "is there a scrollbar" — it reports the overflow extent even on a non-scroll-container, so it read 140 in every candidate treatment and proved nothing; writing `scrollLeft` and reading it back is the only reliable signal. (b) `overflow-x: clip` beside a scrolling axis COMPUTES TO `hidden`, so `overflow-clip-margin` is ignored and the tooltip is cut off instead of escaping. (c) the y-scroller is load-bearing: the rail's content is 764px tall, so a 700px window — an ordinary 13" browser window — already needs 104px of scroll to reach the last item, and simply dropping `overflow-y-auto` would make nav items unreachable. SO THE TOOLTIP LABELS BELOW THE AVATAR, which is where the rail was already labelling everything else — every nav tile has its caption underneath, and the avatar's side flyout was the odd one out. Centred on a 92px rail a 58px tooltip cannot overflow either axis, and it lands in the 32px gap above the first nav item. The odd one out became consistent and the scrollbar has no cause left: clean at 1440/1024/768/390 on three routes, y-scrolling intact. NOTE THIS IS THE SECOND TIME this tooltip's `left-full` has cost horizontal scroll — the first took the DOCUMENT into 42px at 768px from the mobile top bar, fixed then by limiting it to the rail presentation, which relocated the problem instead of removing it. | 
| 342 | THE REVIEW CONSOLE'S TIERING WAS BACKWARDS, AND THE SYMPTOM READ AS A BROKEN LINK. `/admin/review` was lead-only and `router.replace`d the supervisor tier to `/admin/mentor`, so from a supervisor's seat "Review" on the Needs-review queue card — and the record's own "Review profile" — landed on the mentor's RECORD: a page that describes an established mentor, reported by the product owner as "as if the user already exists". The profile under review is not approved yet, so there was nothing there to see and no way to approve it or send it back. A supervisor sits ABOVE a lead mentor in this product's tiering — the same `isSupervisorTier` predicate already grants `canActOnCare`, the heavier of the two permissions — so denying them a review a lead can perform was inverted. The gate is now `persona === "lead-mentor" || isSupervisorTier(persona)`, with review actions included; `new-mentor` and `mentor` still go to their own profile, since no mentor should approve a profile and least of all their own. CHROME FOLLOWS THE PERSON, NOT THE ROUTE. `ReviewShell` mirrors `StaffShell` in `admin/mentor/page.tsx`, which solved this same problem for the record: the lead reviews inside `MentorShell`, the console tiers keep `AdminShell`. Leaving the page on `MentorShell` would have swapped a supervisor's entire left rail the moment they opened a review — the milder version of the bug being fixed. Harness view `G1s` was added because `G1` (lead) stopped covering this route the moment supervisors were admitted: it is a different rail, top bar and responsive break, and axe reports 0 on both. | 
| 343 | THE TASKS SHIFT DIGEST (§7) IS REMOVED IN FULL, AND ONE PIECE OF ITS STATE IS DELIBERATELY KEPT WRITTEN-BUT-UNREAD. "Since you were last here —" sat above the domain tabs and summarised what had moved during an 8-hour gap. Removed at the product owner's request, 2026-08-31, with its component, its unit test, and the two bindings that existed only to feed it: `head` (the digest's own `nextUp` copy — the one at the empty-tab redirect is untouched) and `openItem` (the cross-tab jump; its only caller was the digest's CTA, since the Respond strip navigates by link and takes no callback). `tasksLastVisitAt` STAYS WRITTEN. It is persisted store state at schema v37, and deleting it would cost a migration to remove a number that is cheap to keep and is the exact input any later "what changed while you were away" treatment needs. Written and not read is the deliberate choice here, and it is commented as such so it does not read as a leftover. `openItem`'s shape is recorded in the commit rather than left as an unused seam — any future cross-tab jump wants precisely it. | 
| 344 | THE COMMITTED A11Y BASELINE WAS 30 COMMITS STALE, WHICH MADE EVERY COMPARISON AGAINST IT WRONG IN THE FLATTERING DIRECTION. `docs/responsive/A11Y-BASELINE.json` was last regenerated at `abb3369` and recorded 222 violations. Refreshed 2026-08-31: 79, across 68 views × 9 viewports, 0 harness errors. THE −143 IS NOT ONE CHANGE'S CREDIT, and reading it as such is the trap: two entire rules vanish in that delta and neither belongs to this session's work — `scrollable-region-focusable` (26 → 0) was fixed by `38e5ca4` "keyboard-scrollable preview", and `target-size` (1 → 0) by `83026c6` "the target-size tail", both landed long before and never reflected in the artifact. WHAT TODAY ACTUALLY MOVED is inside the `color-contrast` delta (195 → 79) and is legible by view: every view mounting `MentorProfilePreview` went to zero (A3 35→0, D10 17→0, A4/A4r/E1/G1/G2 11→0 each, H17 9→0, H8 3→0) which is rows 337/338's light-lock and scrim, and G3 9→0 which is row 340's doodle green. THE PROCESS DEFECT IS THE FINDING. A baseline is only evidence if it is regenerated with the work it measures; a stale one still LOOKS current, and it silently inflates the next person's improvement. Worse, a PARTIAL sweep overwrites it with a smaller number that looks like progress — that happened during this session (`ONLY=G,D10,D11,H8,A2` wrote a total of 9 over a full run's 222) and was caught only by reading the diff before staging it. RECOMMEND: regenerate in the same commit as any a11y fix; make `axe.mjs` refuse to write the baseline when `ONLY` is set, or write partial runs to a separate scratch path; and stamp `generated` (it currently reads "unstamped", so the file carries no evidence of its own age — which is why the staleness had to be found through `git log`). REMAINING 79, BY VIEW: B4 16, H2 9, H7 9, B9/B10/B11 8 each, B5/B6/B8 6 each, B12 3. Concentrated at `lg` (23) and `xl` (22), so it is a wide-viewport cluster rather than a token defect spread evenly — the shape row 2's 878-violation token bug had, and this is not that. |
